# Session Log — 2026-07-31

## Summary
WP-4 Integration Readiness Review executed (architectural decision only, no code). Verdict: **READY WITH ONE PREREQUISITE**.

## Completed — WP-4 Integration Readiness Review
**Report:** `engineering/verification/reports/2026-07-31-wp4-integration-readiness-review.md` (7 deliverables).

- **The decisive fact, verified in code:** what crosses the context boundary is **`InboxMessage` carrying a raw payload array** — never a domain object, never the producer's event class. `DeterminationIssuedReactionHandler::handle(InboxMessage)` imports **only** its own context + Shared; a repo-wide grep for consumers importing a producer's `Domain\Events` returns **zero**. And the rule is **machine-enforced**, not aspirational: Deptrac treats cross-context as a violation by omission, in fail mode since PB-007 7D.
- **The producer-side hydrator's true role clarified:** `OutboxEventProcessor:141` is the **only** production caller of `hydratorFor()` — it reconstructs the producer's own event *inside the producing context* for event-bus dispatch. `ChallengeRoutedHydrator` therefore never crosses into the consumer, which is exactly why no consumer imports it. It is producer-side infrastructure, not a shared contract type.
- **Published-language review (unmodified):** no producer leakage · no consumer information embedded · still minimal (three facts, pinned by test) · context-neutral. **Observation, not a defect:** no current consumer needs `routedTo` — it is **producer-justified, not consumer-driven**, which is the correct reason for a published fact to exist.
- **Consumer responsibility map produced** (ownership, not classes): 8 of 10 responsibilities already exist — PM · local `ChallengeRef` · store · **both** duplicate-handling seats (inbox dedupe **and** PM idempotency) · `fromConsumed` · transport/ordering/audit continuity. Two are WP-4's.
- **THE PREREQUISITE (G-2) — the one finding that is not merely unwritten code:** PB-005's **F-1 ruling** binds — *the Domain never names messaging outcomes*; business conditions translate to inbox markers at one Application boundary. Contestation satisfies it (`ChallengeReactionOutcomeTranslator` + markers). **Adjudication has no equivalent**, and its PM entry points are `void` idempotent no-ops returning nothing a translator could read. WP-4 must decide that seam **before** writing the handler — because a PM taught messaging vocabulary cannot be un-taught without rework. No new ADR, no redesign: the rule and the precedent both exist.
- **5 risks recorded**, each with the mitigation already in place (Deptrac catches the classic first-integration mistake at the gate, not at review; "Registration ≠ Delivery" keeps the producer ignorant of consumers; the minting allowlist prevents a second mint).
- **Verdict: READY WITH ONE PREREQUISITE** — G-2 decided as WP-4's first act.

## Decisions
- None taken by this commission (readiness assessment only, as commissioned).

## Next Steps
**ARB ruling on the readiness verdict.** On acceptance, WP-4 opens with G-2 (the translation seam) as its first act, then RED. WP-3A still awaits its own slice acceptance; WP-3B remains deferred on the existence of a routing application service.

## Completed — Cross-Context Integration Contract Audit (six phases; contract only, no code)

**Contract:** `docs/architecture/Cross_Context_Integration_Contract.md` — marked **DESCRIPTIVE** (derived from implementation, not invented); elevating any clause to normative status is a separate decision it does not take.

- **Phase 1 — all chains, not one.** Exactly **three** consumers exist (`implements InboxHandler`): Election←`DeterminationIssued` · Contestation←`DeterminationIssued` · Contestation←`ElectionCorrectionApplied`. All three take `handle(InboxMessage)`.
- **Phase 2 — nine invariants discovered with evidence (R-1..R-9)**, incl. the two that matter most: **R-1** no consumer imports another context's `\Domain` (exhaustive 4×3 context-pair scan: **zero**) and **R-2** no consumer imports another context's **Infrastructure either** — `grep "Hydrator"` in consumer Application layers: **zero**. **R-3:** the hydrator is **producer-side** — `OutboxEventProcessor:141` is its *only* production caller. R-1 is **machine-enforced**: Deptrac treats cross-context as violation-by-omission, fail mode, 0 violations. The contract is already executable architecture.
- **Phase 3 — boundary object lifecycle** drawn end-to-end, showing precisely where each representation lives and that **only the payload + `InboxMessage` cross**.
- **Phase 4 — dependency verification: ZERO violations, zero architectural debt** across all three chains.
- **Phase 5 — THE AUDIT'S MOST VALUABLE CATCH.** The proposed contract answer *"Who reconstructs? Consumer (hydrator → local VOs)"* is **wrong on the mechanism**. Consumers reconstruct **directly from payload primitives** (`DeterminationId::fromString($this->stringField($message->payload, …))`) and **never touch a hydrator**. Had WP-4 followed the proposed answer, Adjudication would have imported `Contestation\Infrastructure\Outbox\ChallengeRoutedHydrator` — the codebase's **first cross-context Infrastructure dependency**, failing Deptrac. The audit prevented exactly the class of error it exists to prevent.
- **Second correction:** a local `RoutedTo` VO was proposed; **rejected** — `routedTo` is a plain producer string and **no consumer needs it** (`openFor()` takes only the challenge identity). Creating it would be a component without authority.
- **Phase 6 — the contract**, answering all eight governing questions; the sharpest clause: *what may never cross* now explicitly includes **the producer's Infrastructure, including hydrators**.
- **Consequence: WP-4 introduces no new architectural concept** — one `InboxHandler` + its registration, the fourth instance of an established, machine-enforced pattern.
- **One observation recorded, not recommended:** a dedicated fitness test for R-2/R-3 would make the *reason* legible, though Deptrac already covers the risk.

## Completed — Cross-Context Integration Contract STRATEGIC VALIDATION (recommendation only, no code)

**Report:** `engineering/verification/reports/2026-07-31-cross-context-contract-strategic-validation.md`. Verdict: **PARTIALLY NORMATIVE — with an unexpected partition.**

- **THE FINDING THAT REFRAMED THE COMMISSION: most of R-1..R-9 are ALREADY normative.** R-1/R-4/R-9 = **ADR-T16** · R-6 = **ADR-MP-06** + PB-006's closure ruling · R-7 = PB-006's permanent *Registration ≠ Delivery* principle · R-8 = **ADR-T3/T4**. Elevating them would create **second homes for frozen rules** — violating the project's own *"the ubiquitous language lives once."* Their correct relationship to the contract is **pointer, not policy**.
- **R-1's strategic grounding is far deeper than convention.** The ratified baseline states *"Zero Partnership/Shared Kernel anywhere is not a stylistic preference — it is a consequence of the domain's accountability requirement… constitutional trust requires every trust-bearing crossing to be attributable: who owed what to whom. Jointly-owned models blur precisely that attribution."* So R-1 follows from **constitutional attributability**, not from the current code — and Shared Kernel is not a counter-example but a **pre-agreed ARB gate** with a recorded falsifiability condition.
- **Exactly ONE clause earns elevation: R-2** (no consumer imports another context's **Infrastructure**, including its hydrators). Deptrac enforces it; **no ADR states it** — ADR-T16 speaks only of Domain/aggregate types. And WP-4 came within one design decision of violating it.
- **Two clauses deliberately held BACK from elevation:** **R-3** (hydrator is producer-side) is **contingent on the current relay design** — elevating it would freeze today's mechanism as law; **R-5** ("primitives only") would **over-constrain**: its strategic content is R-1 restated, and a literal reading makes legitimate structured payloads (Evidence metadata; ADR-T22's array) violations.
- **Counter-example analysis found exactly one genuine exception, and it lands on a clause already classified tactical** (R-5, external/third-party payload formats). ACL, Customer–Supplier, legacy strangler, long-running workflows, multi-producer, and version evolution all conform unchanged.
- **SECOND MATERIAL FINDING — the contract's SCOPE must be stated.** Applying it to future contexts: Evidence ✔ (R-5 strained) · Voting ✔ (anonymity reinforces it) · Appointment ✔ · **Governance ✘ — a scope limitation, not a violation.** Q-1 rules Adjudication consumes Governance's authority decision *"through a published contract"* whose **shape is explicitly undecided** and may be **request/response**. Every rule in the contract presumes an `InboxMessage`. **The contract governs event-carried asynchronous integration only** — left implicit, a future engineer would either force Governance into an event shape or read the contract as violated.
- **Strategic weakness recorded, not elevated:** the contract says how a crossing *works*, never how one is *retired* — v1-retirement in WP-1 was a slice decision under ADR-T5, not a stated rule. Deprecation is real future work.
- **Proposed ARB decision (7 items):** elevate R-2 only, ideally as a **succession-style annotation to ADR-T16** extending its scope from Domain types to any cross-context import (so the rule still lives once) · convert six clauses to pointers · mark R-3/R-5 tactical with reasons · add the scope statement + note Governance's open integration-style question · record retirement + the optional fitness test without ruling · **WP-4 may proceed.**

## Next Steps
**ARB ruling on the seven-item proposal.** WP-4 remains unblocked either way: every rule it must obey is already normative or already machine-enforced, and its prerequisite G-2 is untouched by this review.

## Completed — Cross-Context Contract LAYER CLASSIFICATION (governance classification only, no code)

**Report:** `engineering/verification/reports/2026-07-31-cross-context-contract-layer-classification.md`. **Result: the contract should own NOTHING except its own scope — it becomes a navigation document.**

- **Phase 5 was run FIRST, as the discriminating instrument** (*"assume the transport, hydrators, platform and relay are all replaced — which invariants survive?"*): **survive → strategic** = R-1, R-2, R-7, R-9 + R-4's core (*"the consumer, not the producer, assigns meaning"*); **become vacuous → infrastructure** = R-3 (hydrators would not exist), R-5 (under protobuf/Avro "primitives" means schema types).
- **It refined the previous review on one point:** R-8 is not "strategic with tactical phrasing" but **infrastructure-DERIVED** — consumer idempotency exists *because* delivery is at-least-once (ADR-T3). Change the guarantee, change the requirement.
- **Phase 2 found two governance defects of OPPOSITE kinds:**
  - **R-6 has THREE homes** each restating the invariant — `ADR-MP-Messaging-Platform.md` · `EventProvenance.php` docblock · `developer_guide/audit_system/08_event_provenance.md` (plus PB-006's closure). **Duplication.** Canonical owner = ADR-MP-06; the other two should point.
  - **R-2 has NO home at all** — no ADR states it, yet **Deptrac enforces it** in fail mode. **The inverse defect: a test without a rule.** Its *reason* is unrecorded, so a future engineer could relax the config believing it over-strict. **This is the one real governance gap.**
- **Phase 3 — over-elevation ran the opposite way to expectations:** no strategic rule was secretly tactical; instead **three infrastructure mechanisms sat inside a document that reads like a boundary law** (hydrators, payload shape, idempotency). The useful content of R-3 is really R-2 — strategic for a different reason (dependency direction, not hydrators).
- **Phase 4 — every mechanism is replaceable and each already has an enduring concept recorded in an ADR:** outbox → *state and announcement commit atomically* (T3) · inbox → *effectively-once consumption* (T4) · relay → *eventual delivery with retry* · dispatcher → *deterministic resolution + consumer isolation* (MP-06) · `InboxMessage` → *the crossing carries data, not model* (= R-1/R-9) · **hydrator → no enduring concept at all**, the most replaceable of all. Policy attaches to concepts; mechanisms stay replaceable.
- **Governance matrix produced:** 4 strategic (+R-4's core) · 1 tactical (R-6) · 3 infrastructure (R-3, R-5, R-8) · **the contract owns exactly one thing — its own scope** (event-carried asynchronous integration).
- **Two navigation observations recorded without moving anything:** R-6's canonical home is an *infrastructure* ADR hosting a *tactical* rule (what a domain event may contain) · R-7's is a *PB ticket record* hosting a *strategic* rule. Frozen decisions stay put; the map compensates.
- **Six-item recommendation:** contract → navigation document · **assign R-2 a canonical home (the real gap)** via an ADR-T16 succession annotation · retire R-6's two restatements into pointers · keep R-3/R-5/R-8 at Layer 3 with their enduring concepts named · record the two navigation observations · **WP-4 unaffected** (its rules are Layer-1 already-normative or Layer-3 already-implemented; G-2 is a Layer-2 tactical decision this classification leaves intact).

## Next Steps
**ARB ruling on the combined governance model** (seven-item strategic proposal + six-item layer recommendation). WP-4 remains unblocked; G-2 remains its first act.

### WP-4 Post-Implementation Contract Validation (ARB commission) — theory became evidence

**Verdict: the Cross-Context Integration Contract is EMPIRICALLY VALIDATED.** Nine invariants predicted, nine observed, **none forced** — the handler was written from the process manager's needs and conformance fell out. Strongest evidence is negative: the predicted near-miss (importing the producer's hydrator) did not happen, Deptrac 0.

**Two prediction divergences found — the contract was not perfect:**
- **D-1:** §5 specified provenance via `fromConsumed()`. **Observed: no provenance call at all** — WP-4 publishes nothing. §5 silently assumed every consumer *reacts by publishing*, true of the three consumers it was derived from, false of the first one built after it. The invariant (R-6) is unharmed; the extrapolated *spec row* over-reached. **Derived rules survived first contact; extrapolated rows did not.**
- **D-2:** R-8's second idempotency seat is the **process manager**, not the handler/aggregate its derivation recorded. Rule holds, scope is broader.

**G-2 independently confirmed by evidence, not just argument:** the house translator's signature is `toInboxOutcome(Throwable $businessCondition): Throwable` — it classifies *business conditions* into inbox markers, and WP-4's handler raises none. Nothing to translate.

**One absence reclassified as a future extension point with an armed reversal condition:** WP-4's fail-loud property is **inherited from WP-3A's hydrator**, not owned by Adjudication. If a `ChallengeRouted` producer ever publishes without hydrator-side validation, a generic `InvalidArgumentException` would be classified *transient* and retried forever — so Adjudication would then need its own `PermanentInboxFailure`.

**The material finding came from asking "what PROTECTS this contract?" rather than "is it correct?":**
- **F-1:** `EventRegistryCompletenessTest::PRODUCED_EVENT_TYPES` lists **2 of 4** actually-produced event types (missing `ElectionCorrectionApplied` and `ChallengeRouted`). Behaviour is correct — both hydrators *are* registered — so it is a guard-coverage gap, not a defect. **`ChallengeRouted`'s omission is mine, from WP-3A.** Second occurrence of the AD-004 class: *hand-maintained fitness lists drift, and drift green.*
- **F-2:** nothing asserts a handler is registered **at all** (`InboxHandlerRegistryWiringTest` only checks singleton-ness) — a consumer could compile, pass every gate and silently never consume. The inbox mirror of the hydrator completeness test is missing.

**Canonical pattern established with an explicit discriminator:** Shape A *react-and-publish* (the three earlier consumers: outbox + provenance + translator) vs Shape B *react-only* (**WP-4, the first**: process manager only). One question — *does the reaction produce a fact?* — determines outbox, provenance and translator together.

Report: `engineering/verification/reports/2026-07-31-wp4-post-implementation-contract-validation.md`. **No production code changed, no ADR created, contract not redesigned.**

### ARB refinements to the validation — claim narrowed, dimension test run, F-1/F-2 filed as work items

**1. Claim softened (accepted verbatim):** *"the first implementation provides **strong empirical support** for the contract"* — not "empirically validated". Scope note added naming what is **not** yet exercised: asynchronous compensation · competing consumers · long-running orchestration · consumer-owned version negotiation. One nuance recorded: **multiple consumers of one event IS already exercised** (`DeterminationIssued` has two) — but as *derivation* evidence, not as a post-contract test, so it does not widen the claim.

**2. "Are these the only dimensions that vary?" — tested by enumeration, and the answer changed the classification.** compensate → **excluded by ADR-T8** (choreography, *"not a saga and performs no compensation"*) · schedule → **clock-triggered, out of scope** (WP-6's finality evaluator is explicitly *"scheduled policy … NO event"*) · command → **collapses into Shape A** (R-1/R-2 forbid reaching in, so it must publish) · ignore → **already inside Shape B** (PM-1 no-ops; an outcome, not a shape) · **projection → EXISTS TODAY AND IS UNGOVERNED BY THIS CONTRACT.**

**The material finding:** the platform runs **two parallel event-consumption mechanisms with two independent dedupe stores** — the governed inbox path (exactly four `InboxHandler`s, all correction-loop) and an older Laravel listener/worker projection path (`CommitteeGovernanceProjector`, `CommitteeMemberProjectionListener`, `MemberDirectoryProjector`, `FeePaidProjection`) with its **own** `ProcessedEventModel` dedupe. **This is concrete evidence for the scope statement the strategic validation recommended on theoretical grounds** — the second mechanism is exactly what an unscoped reading would wrongly claim authority over. Classification therefore narrowed as the ARB anticipated: the two shapes are canonical **for the event-carried correction-loop family**, not for all consumers.

**Corroboration found in the roadmap:** WP-6 specifies *"late decisions dead-letter per the ruled policy"* — the very `PermanentInboxFailure` seat WP-4 defended as absent-because-unreachable. The extension point was independently predicted, strengthening the Phase-3 classification.

**3. F-1/F-2 filed as engineering work items, no new document** (ARB direction): **AD-009** (event-registry completeness checks 2 of 4 produced types) and **AD-010** (nothing asserts a handler is registered at all) in the existing `docs/implementation/Architecture_Debt_Backlog.md` — the same register as AD-004, the guard defect whose class AD-009 repeats. Everything else stays architectural knowledge.

### ARB acceptance — validation report is the architectural baseline; two final refinements applied

**Report status recorded (ES-004.3 lifecycle sync):** ✅ **ACCEPTED as the ARCHITECTURAL BASELINE for the correction-loop integration pattern.** The contract is ruled mature enough to govern subsequent **correction-loop** consumers; future implementations continue validating and refining its **scope** rather than assuming universal applicability.

**1. Wording sharpened (my "two parallel mechanisms" understated it).** Now: **"the platform contains two INDEPENDENTLY GOVERNED event-processing models serving different architectural purposes"**, with the seven distinguishing axes tabulated — intent (business collaboration vs read-model maintenance) · path · ownership · correctness criterion (*happened exactly once* vs *converges*) · dedupe store · lifecycle · governed-by. **The clinching axis is rebuildability:** a projection may be discarded and replayed (`GovernanceProjectionRebuilder` exists); a business reaction may not. Unification is explicitly left un-attempted — it would need its own commission.

**2. The class has a name: GOVERNANCE VERIFICATION DRIFT** — *the architecture stays correct while the mechanisms that verify it quietly become incomplete.* Instances **AD-004** (guards scanning zero files) · **AD-009** (2 of 4) · **AD-010** (registration unverified). Recorded in the debt register with its danger signature (**a green gate** — a failing test announces itself, a shrinking one does not) and the counter-measure common to all three: **a gate must enumerate its subject from the system, never from a constant.** Named as vocabulary + review heuristic, **not** minted as a ruling (R-34 default classification) and **not** given its own document (R-38).

**Architectural work for this milestone is complete.** No further architecture commission is warranted before WP-5.

### 🔒 ARCHITECTURE PHASE CLOSED — EPIC-004 Correction-Loop Integration

**ARB chair's formal closure recorded** in the validation report header and CONTEXT: no further architectural commissions · no additional contract refinement · no more terminology changes · no further governance additions.

> **Reopening condition — binding and singular: future architecture work on this contract requires CONTRADICTORY IMPLEMENTATION EVIDENCE, not additional theoretical analysis.**

Rationale on record: *architecture should evolve because reality demands it, not because more ideas are available.* Absent the closure, architecture drifts back into speculation — the failure mode this milestone spent its discipline avoiding.

**Final refinement applied (the last architecture edit of this phase) — the counter-measure was too broad and would have indicted legitimate code.** Corrected to the ARB's wording: *"verification should derive its scope from authoritative system metadata wherever possible, rather than from manually synchronized inventories."* The principle is **not** "constants are bad" — this codebase has legitimate ones (ADR-T5 schema-version windows, `AdjudicationProcessStatus`'s terminal cases, lifecycle enums). Added the discriminating test: **does the constant DEFINE the thing, or MIRROR a set that lives elsewhere?** `PRODUCED_EVENT_TYPES` mirrors the outbox adapters → derive it; terminal statuses define the terminal set → a constant is correct there.

**Governance Verification Drift deliberately NOT promoted to doctrine** — three occurrences in one subsystem recognize a pattern; they do not constitutionalize it (R-34 default classification · no new document per R-38 · R-39's multi-context promotion bar stands). Recorded as vocabulary + review heuristic, and stopped there.

**ARB acceptances on record:** WP-4 architecture **accepted** · contract **accepted as governing for correction-loop consumers** · verification report **accepted as the architectural baseline** · AD-009/AD-010 **accepted as engineering backlog items** · next authorized work **WP-5 implementation**.

### Repository-state investigation — NOTHING LOST; the alarm was a mid-rebase snapshot

**Verdict: no recovery required.** The earlier inspection caught the repository **inside a running `git pull --rebase`**. `08166acd3` — the HEAD I reported as alarming — was an **intermediate replay position**, not a reset: the rebase had replayed the older F-2 commits and had not yet reached WP-3A onward, so those artifacts genuinely were absent *from that intermediate tree*, exactly as a mid-rebase tree should look.

Decisive reflog evidence: `pull --rebase (start): checkout 7b25bb161` → picks → `rebase (finish): returning to refs/heads/feature/pb003` at `32c496b90`. **No `reset`, no force-push, no branch switch, no detached HEAD, one worktree.**

**All slice commits are reachable under NEW hashes** (rebase replays): WP-3A GREEN `4d1809820`→`b08bdc461` · WP-4 GREEN `08c7d2066`→`c3165ee88` · WP-5 RED `9dbce6e62`→`4cf7192f8`. Branch is **in sync with origin** — the 43-commit push backlog is resolved.

**The ARB's correction is vindicated by evidence, and I withdraw my stronger claim.** I said *"my earlier reports were wrong"*; the correct statement is the narrower one: *the checkout I inspected did not match the state those reports described.* Proof: `git cat-file -e b08bdc461:…/ChallengeRoutedHydrator.php` → **present**. So the WP-3A report, and the AD-009 assessment that depended on it, were accurate for the tree they described. **Method lesson recorded: before concluding a prior report was false, check whether the repository is mid-operation — `git status` reports a rebase in progress and the reflog dates it.**

**Baseline re-established (read-only checks):** WP-4 consumption suite **OK (6 tests, 10 assertions)**; WP-5 RED **9 tests, 8 errors** — still red for the expected reason.

**Two findings the rebase produced, neither damage:**
- **F-A:** every commit hash cited in earlier docs is now stale (the WP-4 validation report cites `08c7d2066`, which no longer exists). Recommend citing **slice names** rather than SHAs in governed artifacts — a rebase invalidates hashes but never slice identity.
- **F-B:** my WP-5 allowlist edit (a tracked-file modification made during the suspended GREEN) was swept into `52b0f4a54` *"F-2 executed — CONTEXT.md pruned"*. Consequence: **WP-5 RED keystone 9 now passes** (0 failures where RED had 1) — part of GREEN landed ahead of authorization, inside an unrelated commit. Recommend accepting in place and noting it at WP-5 completion rather than rewriting history. The 6 untracked WP-5 GREEN files were untouched.

Report: `engineering/verification/reports/2026-07-31-repository-state-investigation.md`. **Read-only throughout: no reset, no rebase, no force-push, no history rewrite, no recovery.**

**EP-03 readiness: GO.** WP-1/WP-2/WP-5 accepted; APM `enforceHorizon()` + store `dueForHorizon($asOf)` + `Expired` state + `Determination::finalize()` all exist from earlier slices; horizon-expiry policy is **RULED** (EPIC-004K §197). **Phase 7 finding: WP-2 built the horizon's mechanism deliberately WITHOUT its duration.** WP-6 supplies the duration and the trigger, nothing more — `dueForHorizon` already takes a caller-supplied cut-off by design.

**Two findings from the existing code, both grounded in ruled policy:**

- **F-1 — `enforceHorizon()` is not MAD-aware and would expire EVERYTHING.** It passes `$now` as the cut-off to `dueForHorizon($asOf)`, which selects `opened_at <= $asOf`; so on first call every non-terminal process is "due", including one opened a second ago. **Inert today** (nothing calls it — WP-6 delivers the scheduler), so this is the WP-2/WP-6 seam behaving exactly as WP-2's docblock documented, not a live defect. WP-6 must pass `now − MAD`, and a RED keystone pins it.
- **F-2 — a late authority decision is currently a silent no-op; §197 rules it a CONFLICT.** `receiveRulingDecision()` treats "already concluded (or expired)" as one branch. That is correct for **redelivery** (ADR-T3) but §197 rules *"a post-expiry authority decision dead-letters as a conflict."* Two situations share one branch and must be separated — a **ruled-policy gap, not an architecture question**. RED keystone 6 guards against over-correcting into a broken idempotency.

**Provenance question flagged, not decided:** the expiry announcement is triggered by a **clock**, not a consumed message — so it may be a **new conversation**, which would make the horizon enforcer a chain origin needing an allowlist entry. Exactly the question WP-5's sharpened invariant now answers precisely. Recorded for the RED boundary; any allowlist change is an ARB decision.

### WP-6 pre-RED clarification RESOLVED — and it changed the slice's scope

**The ARB's criterion answered cleanly:** *does Contestation need to react to Expiry as an external business fact?* **YES, and it is ruled, not inferred** — EPIC-004K §197: *"return to Contestation … the challenge's disposition is Contestation's per its own rules."* A ruling that assigns the disposition to another bounded context **is** a statement that the fact crosses the boundary.

**Classification:** the expiry announcement **is** published language (hydrator + registration, producer-side, per WP-3A's rule), and it **BEGINS a new conversation** — a timer consumes no message, so `fromConsumed()` has nothing to derive from, and *an origin begins a conversation; it never transfers ownership of one.* **This is the invariant applying, not an exception to it:** `route` begins the correction-loop conversation; the horizon enforcer begins the *failure-to-conclude* conversation. Each has exactly one origin. Consequence: the enforcer is a chain origin needing an allowlist entry (ADR-MP-06 → ARB decision).

**⚠️ Applying the criterion exposed a gap that changes WP-6's scope.** Contestation **cannot currently consume** this event:

```php
public function lapse(...) { $this->guard('lapse', ChallengeState::Raised, ChallengeState::Admitted); }  // NOT Routed
```

A challenge whose adjudication expires is in state **`Routed`**, and `lapse()` refuses `Routed`. **There is no `Routed → <disposition>` transition in the Challenge aggregate**, so §197 names a rule whose transition does not yet exist. The publication side is fully within WP-6's authority; the consumption side needs a **Contestation domain change outside WP-6's stated scope**, plus a modelling answer (lapse? re-route? dismissal? new state?) that only the ARB/business can give.

**Recommendation — the WP-3A/WP-4 precedent, already run once successfully here:** WP-6 delivers publication + registration; the consumer becomes a separate slice (**WP-6B**). An announced fact with no consumer is *published language awaiting a consumer*, not a broken loop — the inbox simply resolves no handler. **Not assumed: requested as ARB Decision B.**

**Two ARB decisions requested:** **(A)** add the horizon enforcer to `CHAIN_ORIGIN_ALLOWLIST` · **(B)** the scope split. Noted interaction with WP-5's carried checkpoint: WP-6 may **add** one originator while the checkpoint asks whether another can be **removed** — the allowlist could legitimately end WP-6 with two entries again, a *different* two.

**RED plan amended:** keystone 4 now also asserts the payload is registered and hydratable; keystone 11 becomes concrete (*newly minted correlation, null causation* — a chain start); and **no keystone asserts a Contestation reaction**, per Decision B.

### WP-6 DECISION A analysed — the governing question, reordered ahead of provenance (ARB direction)

**The ARB was right to reorder:** *does an expiry announcement exist as published language at all?* comes before *what provenance does it carry?* And the precedent is **stronger than one instance** — the codebase has **two** event-free temporal transitions: `Challenge::lapse()` (*"the Lapsed timeout emits NO domain event"*) and `Determination::finalize()` (*"Final emits no event"*), both Round 50-07 v1.2.

**But one discriminator explains both precedents and separates this case — WHO BEARS THE CONSEQUENCE:**

| Case | Timer owner | Consequence owner | Crossing needed? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Challenge::lapse()` | Contestation | **Contestation** | No — nothing to tell |
| `Determination::finalize()` | Adjudication | **Adjudication** | No |
| **Adjudication horizon expiry** | Adjudication | **Contestation** (§197) | **Yes** |

Both event-free precedents share a property this case lacks: the context owning the clock also owns the consequence. The horizon is the only one of the three where they differ.

**Falsification test run — every non-event mechanism fails on an existing authority:** Contestation running its own MAD timer duplicates a duration it does not own (Q-2/§81 makes the APM the *enforcer*) · a query reads another context's internal process state (the frozen contract admits only published payloads) · scheduled reconciliation is the ungoverned projection mechanism, not business collaboration · Adjudication calling Contestation inverts dependency direction (R-7).

**Decisive:** if expiry publishes nothing, **§197's ruling cannot be honoured at all** — Contestation would never learn that its routed challenge's adjudication failed to conclude, so the disposition it was assigned could never be exercised. "No publication" does not merely differ from §197; it makes a **ruled policy unimplementable**.

**Recommendation: Decision A = YES, published integration language** — with the `lapse()` precedent **respected, not overridden**, expressed as one rule that explains all three cases without a new concept: **a temporal transition publishes only when the consequence crosses a boundary.** If the ARB rules NO regardless, the honest consequence is recorded: §197 would have to be reopened as a business question, since no conforming delivery mechanism would remain.

**Three ARB decisions now pending in order:** A (publish at all) → B (allowlist entry, if A=YES) → C (scope split: WP-6 publishes, WP-6B consumes — Contestation still has no `Routed → disposition` transition).

### WP-6 Decision A refined — recommendation phrasing restored, discriminator moved onto business responsibility

**1. Phrasing corrected — governance discipline restored.** *"Decision A: YES"* became **"RECOMMENDATION TO THE ARB — Decision A = YES"**, with the reason stated in the plan: *evidence → recommendation → authority decision*; the recommendation does not become the ruling by being well argued, and the analysis has no authority to collapse the two. This is the same discipline that caught my WP-2 mapper overreach and the Option C over-claim.

**2. The generalized rule refined — and the ARB's version is materially better, not just tighter.** Mine was *"publishes only when the consequence crosses a boundary"*; the refined rule is:

> **A temporal transition becomes published integration language only when another bounded context requires that fact to discharge one of its business responsibilities.**

**Why it matters:** boundary-crossing alone is **not sufficient** — projections, analytics, reporting and monitoring all cross boundaries without earning published-language status, and the contract validation already found an entire **ungoverned projection mechanism** doing exactly that. My wording would have wrongly admitted that fourth category. The refined rule covers all four cases: `lapse()` ❌ · `finalize()` ❌ · horizon expiry ✅ · cross-boundary projections ❌. **The discriminator is business responsibility, not topology.**

The comparison table now carries the responsibility column rather than a same/different column, so the reasoning is visible at a glance.

### WP-6 Decision A — necessity constraint added; rule accepted as reasoning, NOT promoted

**The rule's final form (ARB refinement — the necessity test is the operative change):**

> **A temporal transition becomes published integration language only when another bounded context CANNOT discharge one of its assigned business responsibilities without learning that fact.**

*Requires* was still too permissive: it is **not enough that another context would benefit** or be better informed — it must be **unable to fulfil an assigned responsibility without the fact**. That is precisely the shape of the §197 argument (Contestation cannot exercise its assigned disposition while ignorant of the expiry), and it excludes the large class of **merely informative** events, which is where published-language inflation normally begins.

**Promotion restraint recorded, at ARB direction and consistent with the standing freeze:** the rule is accepted **as the reasoning supporting Decision A**, *not* as a project-wide standard; broader applicability must be confirmed across further bounded contexts first. A candidate observation is noted without promotion — **drop the word "temporal"** and the rule generalizes to any business fact (determinations, appointments, evidence, payments). Left un-promoted under **R-38** (concepts enter only on operational evidence) and **R-39** (the multi-context bar, preserved as a rule when the DDD principles got their one-time exception). Filed in the same register as *Governance Verification Drift* — named, useful, deliberately un-constitutionalized.

## Next Steps
**ARB ruling on Decision A** (recommendation: YES), then **B** (provenance / allowlist entry) and **C** (scope split), then RED.

### ARB DECISION A APPROVED — expiry IS published integration language; B direction approved; C is the last gate

**Final rule wording (ARB), with each qualifier earning its place:**

> **A temporal transition becomes published integration language only when another bounded context cannot CORRECTLY discharge one of its EXPLICITLY ASSIGNED business responsibilities without learning that BUSINESS FACT.**

*correctly* excludes convenience dressed as necessity — a context that could act, but would act better informed, fails · *explicitly assigned* forces the responsibility to trace to an architectural authority (here §197 assigns it in so many words) rather than self-declared interest · *business fact* excludes technical notifications. **The rule is strong because it is hard to satisfy** — most cross-context information fails it, which is what keeps published language from inflating.

**Decision A APPROVED** on the four-point justification: an explicitly assigned responsibility exists · it cannot be correctly discharged without the fact · no conforming mechanism delivers it · therefore published language is justified. The generalized rule stands as the **reasoning for this decision only** — promotion bar unchanged (R-38 · R-39).

**The progression is itself the method, and worth recording:** boundary crossing → responsibility → **necessity** → governance restraint.

**Decision B direction approved:** the announcement **begins** a new conversation (newly minted correlation, null causation), so the horizon enforcer joins the allowlist as the origin of the *failure-to-conclude* conversation — following from A rather than as a special case. After WP-6: two originators, each owning exactly one conversation. WP-5's checkpoint separately asks whether the authority-decision originator can leave; either way **the invariant never moves**.

**Decision C is the one remaining gate, and it must precede RED because it fixes WP-6's definition of done.** If WP-6 had to deliver the consumer too, RED would need keystones for a Routed-to-disposition Challenge transition **that does not exist**, whose shape — lapse, re-route, dismissal, new state — only the ARB/business can decide. Discovering that mid-GREEN is precisely the failure this process prevents. Recommendation: approve the split (WP-3A to WP-4 precedent).

## Next Steps
**ARB Decision C** — then **RED begins immediately** (11 keystones already planned; none asserts a Contestation reaction).

### Decision C reframed as an IMPLEMENTATION BOUNDARY decision; cardinality emphasis corrected

**1. C was mis-framed by me, and the ARB's reframing is substantive, not cosmetic.** *"Scope split"* reads as scheduling. The real question is a DDD boundary:

> **Where does WP-6's bounded-context implementation responsibility end?**

**Option A** — timer → expire → **publish → register** ▮END (Adjudication only; mirrors WP-3A, with WP-6B mirroring WP-4).
**Option B** — … → **consume → Challenge transition** ▮END (Adjudication **and** Contestation in one slice; no precedent here).

**Recommendation: Option A, on three DDD grounds rather than convenience:**
1. The two responsibilities belong to **two different bounded contexts** — Adjudication owns *announcing that it failed to conclude*; Contestation owns *what that means for the challenge* (§197). One slice implementing both makes one commit the owner of two contexts' decisions.
2. **Option B would couple the producer's completeness to a consumer's existence** — exactly the coupling **PB-006's *Registration ≠ Delivery*** exists to prevent. WP-6 could not be "done" until Contestation reacted, reintroducing the producer→consumer dependency the platform deliberately removed.
3. Option B would force the `Routed → disposition` **modelling decision inside an implementation commission** — which this plan's Phase 1 forbids (*architectural uncertainty ⇒ stop and report*).

**2. Cardinality emphasis corrected in both places it appeared.** I had written *"two originators after WP-6"*, which invites the wrong inference. Corrected to the ARB's formulation:

> **After WP-6, every integration conversation continues to satisfy "exactly one origin per conversation." Additional conversations may introduce additional origins without violating the invariant.**

The architecture does not prefer two origins, or any number — **the allowlist's size is an artifact of how many distinct conversations exist; it is never itself the rule.** The invariant is about **conversation identity, not system cardinality**.

**Preserved deliberately:** the progression note (*boundary crossing → responsibility → necessity → governance restraint*), because it documents how the decision was actually reached — not as a standard.

## Next Steps
**ARB Decision C** (recommendation: Option A) — then **RED begins immediately** (11 keystones planned; none asserts a Contestation reaction).

### WP-6 RED ACCEPTED — and a governance correction to my own re-scope proposal

**Decisions A/B/C accepted; RED accepted; Phase-15 discipline accepted; F-1 accepted as operational evidence** (before RED it was architectural reasoning — a process opened one minute ago being expired is now measured, not argued).

**The governance correction, and it was mine to make.** I had written *"WP-6C"* and *"WP-6D"* as though those slices existed. RED's evidence establishes only the first of two statements:

| Statement | Established by RED? |
|---|---|
| These capabilities cannot be implemented under today's approved model | ✅ **Yes** |
| Therefore they become WP-6C and WP-6D | ❌ **No — that is an authority decision** |

Amending the roadmap is not the implementation team's to do. The plan now carries a **recommendation with no slice identifiers claimed**: re-scope WP-6 to horizon + expiry announcement, and create follow-on slices *after* the modelling decisions are approved — one opening with the **Demand modelling question** (§80/PM-3), one with the **cross-context question** (§142). Names, numbering and sequencing come from the ARB's amendment. *The implementation team discovered a boundary; the ARB decides how the roadmap changes.*

**Wording tightened at ARB direction:** *"neither blocker is a defect"* → **"neither finding is an implementation defect; both identify architectural capabilities that have not yet been MODELLED."** *"Absence"* can read as accidental; *"not yet modelled"* names the architectural reason.

**Kept deliberately:** *"passes for the wrong reason."* The ARB asked for it verbatim, and it carries a real engineering idea — a passing test can be meaningful, vacuous, accidental, inherited or masked, and only classification tells them apart.

## Next Steps
**ARB ruling on the re-scope recommendation**, then GREEN. WP-3A and WP-4 slice acceptances also remain open.

### WP-6 re-scope recommendation restated in three separated layers; identifiers withheld

**Recommendation now separates observed evidence · architectural interpretation · authority action**, so no layer can be mistaken for another:

1. **Observed evidence (from RED):** the process state has `admittedEvidence` and `returnForMoreEvidence()` but **no** demand entity, **no** per-demand deadline and **no** column for either; §142's *"no challenge open against it"* is a Contestation aggregate (`TargetType::Determination`) that Adjudication cannot read.
2. **Architectural interpretation:** the ubiquitous language **lacks three concepts** — *Evidence Demand*, *Evidence Demand Deadline*, and *finality evaluation across a bounded-context boundary*. **Modelling gaps, not coding defects.**
3. **Authority action (the ARB's alone):** *if the ARB agrees with this analysis*, it may amend the roadmap by re-scoping WP-6 and introducing follow-on slices after the modelling decisions are approved.

**Follow-on work is now referred to descriptively — "a follow-on slice for evidence-demand deadlines" — with no identifier claimed.** Naming them would let this document imply a decision that has not been made. Formal names, numbering and sequencing arise only from the ARB's amendment.

**Append-only discipline honoured (ES-004.2):** the two earlier session-log entries that named "WP-6C/WP-6D" are **NOT rewritten** — history is never edited to manufacture consistency. The correction is recorded forward, in this entry and in the plan's own correction record (which must name what it corrects). `CONTEXT.md` — a Runtime artifact, so mutable — is corrected in place.

**Observation recorded, deliberately NOT promoted:** the correction is another instance of a separation this program keeps converging on — *discovery produces evidence · architecture produces recommendations · governance produces decisions · implementation executes decisions.* Filed as an observation in the same register as *Governance Verification Drift* and the published-language necessity rule: useful, repeatedly demonstrated, and left un-constitutionalized pending multi-context evidence (R-38 · R-39).

## Next Steps

### WP-6 ARCHITECTURAL NORMALIZATION — findings classified; four inconsistencies found in my own document

A consistency pass, not a redesign. **Four inconsistencies found and corrected textually:**

- **N-1 — findings carried no common taxonomy.** F-1/F-2, the two blocked items, and the two Phase-15 corrections sat in three separate sections with nothing saying which *kind* each was. A finding's kind determines its **authority path**, so without the taxonomy a reader cannot tell which findings are fixable inside an implementation commission.
- **N-2 — "neither finding is an implementation defect" read as contradicting F-1/F-2, which ARE implementation gaps.** Both statements were true of *different* findings; the apparent contradiction was created by the missing taxonomy, not by an error of fact. Corrected **at source** by scoping the sentence, so a re-read cannot resurrect it.
- **N-3 — the two blocked items were grouped as "two capabilities."** They are **different categories requiring different authorities**: 🔷 a **Domain Modelling Gap** (Adjudication lacks *Evidence Demand* / *Evidence Demand Deadline* — tactical DDD, no crossing) vs 🔶 an **Integration Contract Gap** (finality needs Contestation's state — strategic DDD, a new crossing, possibly query-shaped, which the frozen contract explicitly does not govern). **One creates concepts; one creates collaboration.** Conflated, they would force one ADR to mint concepts *and* design a crossing in a single vote.
- **N-4 — evidence was not split by kind.** Now every gap cites **repository evidence** (missing entity/VO/column) *and* **behavioural evidence** (RED cannot proceed without inventing concepts). Two independent forms of proof are stronger than one undifferentiated "observed evidence".

**Every finding now carries exactly one of four categories** — Implementation Gap (F-1, F-2) · Domain Modelling Gap · Integration Contract Gap · Test Gap (the two Phase-15 diagnoses) — each with its repository evidence, behavioural evidence, and **authority required**. F-1/F-2 require **no new authority** (Q-2/§81 and §197 already rule them), which is precisely why they are GREEN-ready while the other two are not.

**Governance verified rather than assumed:** no work-package identifier appears outside the correction record that disowns it (grep-checked, two hits, both inside it) · every recommendation reads *"Recommendation to the ARB"* · no new domain concept is defined, only named as missing · historical entries not rewritten.

**Readiness: the package is architecturally normalized and READY FOR GREEN for the two Implementation Gaps only.** The 🔷 and 🔶 gaps each await their own authority and cannot be resolved by writing code.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN (MAD-aware horizon + expiry announcement). Nothing further in the planning package requires architectural work.

### WP-6 DDD NORMALIZATION — the requested "DDD layer" column immediately found a real defect

**N-5 — the late-decision exception was in the wrong architectural layer, in my own RED test.** I imported `Domain\Exception\LateDecisionOnExpiredAdjudication`. The house places **process** exceptions in `Application\Process\Exception\` (`IllegalProcessTransition`) and **aggregate** exceptions in `Domain\Exception\` (`DeterminationAlreadyIssued`, `DeterminationNotFound`).

**DDD justification:** *"a late decision arrived for an expired process"* is a rule about the **process manager's lifecycle**, not about the `Determination` aggregate's invariants. WP-2 deliberately seated the APM and its state machine in the **Application** layer; its exceptions must follow, or the Domain layer acquires a concept belonging to orchestration. Corrected; **RED re-confirmed unchanged** (10 tests · 2 errors · 5 failures).

**This is precisely what the ARB's requested column was for.** The four-category taxonomy classified *what* each finding was and said nothing about *where it belongs* — so it could not have caught a misplacement. One added column, one real defect found.

**Every finding now carries four attributes, one each:** taxonomy · DDD owner/layer · authority path · resolving artifact. The artifact always matches the authority — **code** where a rule already exists (F-1, F-2, expiry announcement), an **ADR + domain model** where a concept must be minted (🔷 Evidence Demand), a **context map + ADR** where a relationship must be designed (🔶 finality), a **test suite** for verification gaps.

**Phase 5 verified item by item, with one nuance worth recording:** the expiry announcement introduces a new **event name** for an **existing** concept (`Expired` — §57, `AdjudicationProcessStatus::Expired`). **Naming an existing fact is not minting a new one**, which is why it clears the "no new ubiquitous language" test while Evidence Demand does not.

**Readiness: DDD-NORMALIZED and architecturally ready for GREEN.** One taxonomy, one owner, one authority path, one artifact per finding; GREEN contains only already-authorized implementation work.

**Observation recorded, not promoted:** the five questions this pass converged on (*what kind · where · who owns · what artifact · can we proceed*) join the un-promoted register alongside Governance Verification Drift, the published-language necessity rule, and discovery-does-not-create-architecture. **N-5 is the first evidence in their favour** — the fourth question found a defect the first four categories missed. Elevation waits for multi-work-package evidence (R-38 · R-39).

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN (MAD-aware horizon + expiry announcement + late-decision conflict). No architectural work remains in the planning package.

### WP-6 OWNERSHIP VERIFICATION — two more inconsistencies, both from leaving ownership implicit

**O-1 — the two blocked items had a LAYER but no OWNER.** I had assigned 🔷 to *"Domain Model / Aggregate"* and 🔶 to *"Context Mapping"*, which reads as though an owner exists. **Neither does:** the aggregate that would own *Evidence Demand* is not modelled, and the Adjudication ↔ Contestation relationship has **no owner for a query-shaped crossing** (the frozen contract governs event-carried async integration only). A layer says *where a thing would live*; an owner says *who is answerable*. Owner now recorded as **UNASSIGNED — pending the modelling / integration decision**.

**O-2 — the expiry announcement risked TWO owners:** the Adjudication BC *as producer* and the process manager *as emitter*. The frozen contract settles it — **the producing bounded context owns publication and registration** (R-7), so the BC is the owner and the **APM is the emitter, not the owner**. One owner recorded.

**One split confirmed as correct rather than corrected:** MAD's **duration** is owned by Q-2/ARB while **enforcement** is owned by the APM. That is not a defect — it is Q-2's own rule made visible: *"the APM enforces a duration it does not own."* The artifact matches: an **INTERIM-marked config value naming the decision it awaits**, so the number is visibly not the code's to choose.

**The ownership column produced the sharpest readiness test of any pass:**

> **An item with no business owner cannot be implemented** — not because it is technically hard, but because **nobody would be answerable for the invariant the code would encode.**

That classifies WP-6 mechanically, without judgement calls: horizon ✅ · late-decision conflict ✅ · expiry announcement ✅ (a new *name* for `Expired`, §57 — not a new concept) · 🔷 ❌ (no owner) · 🔶 ❌ (no owner). Where the owner is absent, **the artifact's job is to establish the owner** — which is exactly why code cannot substitute for an ADR.

**Wording corrected at ARB direction:** N-5 is the framework's **first OPERATIONAL VALIDATION**, not "first evidence in its favour" — it detected a defect the previous framework *structurally could not*. Stronger than anecdote; still below the promotion threshold (R-38 · R-39). The three stages — **taxonomy → layer → ownership** — stay in the un-promoted register.

**Readiness: DDD-normalized, ownership-verified, ready for GREEN.** Every GREEN finding carries one taxonomy, one layer, one business owner, one authority path, one resolving artifact.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN. No architectural or ownership work remains in the planning package.

### WP-6 OWNERSHIP SEMANTICS — three ambiguities, the first a correction to my own correction

**O-3 — my previous O-2 resolution collapsed a distinction instead of resolving it.** I wrote *"one owner — the BC; the APM is the emitter, not the owner"*, treating them as **competing**. They are **complementary**: the **Adjudication BC** owns the *meaning* of "adjudication expired" (only a bounded context can own a business fact's meaning); the **APM** owns *performing* the publication. Recording one erases a real accountability. Now recorded as **business owner + execution owner**.

**O-4 — 🔷 Evidence Demand was marked UNASSIGNED on both axes, which OVERSTATED the gap.** Its **execution owner is already assigned**: EPIC-004K **PM-3** gives the APM *"express evidence demands and track them to satisfaction or deadline."* The executor is known; the **concept** is not — and that asymmetry is exactly what makes this a **Domain Modelling Gap** rather than an execution gap. There is someone to run it and nothing yet to run.

**O-5 — 🔶 Finality was marked UNASSIGNED flatly, which UNDERSTATED what exists.** Finality's *legality* **is** owned — `Determination::finalize()` guards `Issued → Final`, and Phase 3 already recorded *"the Determination aggregate owns finality's legality."* What is unowned is **the collaboration**: answering *"is a challenge open against this determination?"* across a boundary. That precision is what distinguishes an **Integration Contract Gap** from a domain gap — the invariant has an owner; the **crossing** does not.

**O-4 and O-5 are errors in opposite directions**, both caught by the same added dimension — which is the strongest argument yet that responsibility type carries independent information.

**A sharper readiness test emerged:** 🔷 has an execution owner and is *still* excluded from GREEN, because **an assigned executor does not substitute for an unowned concept.**

**Consistency verified:** a bounded context owns business meaning · process managers own orchestration · aggregates own invariants · context mappings own collaboration · tests own verification. The one place two accountabilities meet — the expiry announcement — is recorded as **Business + Execution with two named owners**, not one absorbing the other.

**No future owner invented.** `UNASSIGNED` appears only where no accountable owner exists today, with the reason named. *A future aggregate is an architectural destination, never an owner.*

**Operational validations to date: three** — N-5 (layer found a misplacement) · O-1 (ownership found a layer with no owner) · O-4/O-5 (responsibility type found ownership recorded at the wrong granularity, in both directions). **Still un-promoted:** all three come from **one** work package, and the bar asks for evidence across several (R-38 · R-39).

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN. No architectural, ownership or semantic work remains in the planning package.

### WP-6 SEMANTIC NORMALIZATION — three leaks closed; review framework FROZEN

**Three semantic inconsistencies found, each a dimension answering ANOTHER dimension's question:**
- **S-1** 🔷's Responsibility Type read *"Business (future)"* — *"future"* is a **GREEN-readiness** statement smuggled into a responsibility classification. Corrected to **Business**; timing lives in its own column.
- **S-2** 🔷's DDD Layer read *"Domain (destination)"* — *"destination"* smuggles an **ownership** statement into a location classification. Layer answers *where it belongs*, which is knowable even when nothing lives there; that nothing does is already carried by Business Owner = UNASSIGNED. Corrected to **Domain**.
- **S-3** F-1's Business Owner held **two owners in one cell** (*"Adjudication BC · duration parameter: Q-2/ARB"*) — and duplicated the MAD row. One dimension, one answer: corrected to **Adjudication BC**.

**One redundancy noted and deliberately NOT corrected:** taxonomy *Test Gap* maps 1:1 to responsibility type *Verification*. They answer different questions and merely coincide here; collapsing them would lose the distinction everywhere else. **A 1:1 mapping is not an overlap.**

**The framework is now two groups of four, one question each:** **Architecture** (Taxonomy · DDD Layer · Business Owner · Responsibility Type) and **Execution** (Execution Responsibility Owner · Authority · Artifact · GREEN Readiness).

**Rename adopted:** *Execution Owner* → **Execution Responsibility Owner.** Beside *Business Owner* the old name read as a **competing** owner; the new one makes the different-responsibilities point structurally — O-3's finding now lives in the vocabulary rather than in a footnote.

**🔒 FRAMEWORK FROZEN, at ARB recommendation, and I think this is the right call.** Three operational validations (N-5 · O-1 · O-4/O-5) all come from **one** work package. The framework grew a dimension in each of the last three passes; a fourth would be refinement without new information. **It is now used unchanged, and cross-slice evidence is the only path to promotion (R-38 · R-39). The most valuable property it can now demonstrate is stability, not sophistication.**

*Most portable output, recorded verbatim and still un-promoted:* **an assigned executor does not substitute for an unowned concept** — it stops an implementation team mistaking orchestration for modelling.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN (MAD-aware horizon · late-decision conflict · expiry announcement). No architectural, ownership or semantic work remains.

### WP-6 FINAL FREEZE VALIDATION — two of my own over-generalizations corrected; framework review ENDS

**The ARB caught me generalizing WP-6 observations into framework laws. Both corrections stand.**

**1. "Taxonomy determines Authority" was too strong — it is GOVERNANCE-MEDIATED.** The falsifying test is decisive: change governance so integration decisions go to an Architecture Council instead of the ARB, and **the taxonomy is unchanged while the authority changes.** So taxonomy cannot determine authority. Corrected to **"Taxonomy INFORMS Authority; governance rules mediate"**, with the real chain recorded: Finding to Taxonomy to **Governance Rules** to Authority.

**DDD justification:** taxonomy is a property of the **finding**; authority is a property of the **organisation deciding about it**. Collapsing them embeds an organisational arrangement into an architectural framework — the same category error N-5 corrected in code, where an orchestrator held a bounded context's meaning.

**2. "GREEN = Owner AND Authority AND Artifact" was an equation, and it over-specified.** Those three sufficed *in WP-6* only because Decision C settled the implementation boundary and the ARB settled the scope. **Another slice could have all three and still be blocked** by an unresolved modelling question elsewhere. Corrected to **"GREEN Readiness is derived from the execution dimensions"** — no conjunction asserted. Stating one would generalize a single slice's contingency into a law.

**Every dependency is now classified:** the Group-A chain (Taxonomy to Layer to Business Owner to Responsibility Type) and Authority to Artifact are **structural**; Taxonomy to Authority is **governance-derived**; Owner/Authority/Artifact to GREEN is **implementation-derived**. **The single cross-group link is governance-mediated, not structural** — which keeps the taxonomy useful without making it authoritative: it tells you *which governance rule applies*, never *what that rule says*.

**What the freeze does NOT freeze: today's governance decisions.** Frozen are dimensions, vocabulary and responsibilities. Values — who the authority is, which artifact is approved, what is in scope — change with governance, and the framework absorbs that without amendment. **A framework that broke under substituting the deciding body would have frozen governance, not structure.**

**FRAMEWORK REVIEW ENDS HERE.** Four passes produced N-5, O-1, O-4/O-5 and these two corrections — all from one work package. The next meaningful evidence is **applying it unchanged to independent slices**, not another theoretical improvement. Attention returns to delivery.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** then GREEN (MAD-aware horizon, late-decision conflict, expiry announcement). Nothing else outstanding on WP-6.

### WP-6 TRANSITION TO OPERATIONAL USE — refinement ends, application begins

**Two wording corrections, both accepted:**

**T-1 — "Taxonomy informs Authority" was still one hop short.** Corrected to **"Taxonomy SELECTS the applicable governance RULE; the governance rule IDENTIFIES the authority."** *Informs* still pointed taxonomy at a person or committee; it points at a **policy**. In DDD terms: taxonomy is a property of the finding, a governance rule is a **policy over findings of that kind**, and the authority is whoever that policy appoints. **Two hops, not one** — and the distinction becomes load-bearing the moment governance rules become configurable, because only the middle hop would change.

**T-2 — "Framework review ENDS here" was absolute.** Corrected to **"Framework REFINEMENT ends here; framework APPLICATION begins here."** The framework has changed **phase**, not finished forever. Absolute wording would misdescribe an evidence-driven process as a completed one, and would make a later legitimate challenge look like a violation.

**Four verification phases passed:** all eight dimensions are stable concepts (GREEN Readiness is stable *as a question*; its answer is always slice-specific — which is exactly why the conjunction was removed) · every dependency sits in exactly one category · the freeze covers dimensions/vocabulary/responsibilities and **not** governance bodies, organizational structures, approval assignments or roadmap sequencing · **governance remains external** — the framework *asks* who may authorize, it never *states* who does.

**Governance-change test re-run:** substitute an Architecture Council for the ARB on integration decisions — every dimension asks the same question, the governance **rule** changes, the Authority **value** changes, and **no dimension, name or responsibility requires amendment.**

> **Framework refinement is complete. Future evidence will come exclusively from operational application across independent slices.**

**Four consecutive passes have REDUCED over-generalization rather than added capability** — which is the signal the design phase is genuinely over. Reopening remains singular: a later *independent* slice where the framework fails to classify or expose a real issue.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** — the only remaining gate — then GREEN: MAD-aware horizon, late-decision conflict, expiry announcement.

### WP-6 FRAMEWORK OPERATIONALLY ADOPTED — protocol + template defined; NO framework change

**Confirmed first: no framework change is proposed.** The eight dimensions, their vocabulary, their responsibilities and the governance model are untouched. Only *evidence collection* is defined.

**Protocol — five steps at each work-package closure:** complete the WP → record **one** of four outcomes → record classification failures → test the reopening criteria → recommend *continue unchanged* or *reopen*.

**Four outcomes, closed set:** Exposed a previously unknown defect · Prevented a potential architectural defect · Required no framework changes · **Failed to classify a finding** — the only outcome that can trigger reopening.

**One operational clarification added so "exactly one outcome" stays usable:** where several apply, record the **strongest** (Exposed > Prevented > Required no changes), because a framework that exposed a defect trivially also required no changes, and recording the weaker outcome would **hide the stronger evidence**. *Failed to classify* is recorded whenever it occurs regardless. This is a recording convention, **not** a new category.

**WP-6's own record opened (outcome deferred to closure, since GREEN and acceptance are pending).** Evidence so far: **N-5** — a *committed* RED test imported a **Domain** exception for an **Application-layer process** rule, exposed by the DDD-layer dimension — plus O-1, O-4, O-5 and S-1..S-3, each a mis-record caught **before** GREEN. **Classification failures: none** — every finding placed in exactly one taxonomy, layer, owner and responsibility type, including the two whose owner is legitimately `UNASSIGNED`. Recommendation: **continue unchanged**.

**Independence honoured:** all that evidence was gathered **without** changing a taxonomy, dimension, term or governance rule. *The framework is the measuring instrument, not the subject of measurement.*

**Durable home given, no new document created:** the protocol and template live in the plan; a four-line pointer was added to `.claude/MEMORY.md` so future slices find it without re-deriving it — MEMORY.md being the designated place for cross-session conventions.

> **The framework is operationally adopted. Future confidence will come from repeated successful application across independent work packages, not from additional theoretical refinement.**

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** — the only remaining gate — then GREEN: MAD-aware horizon, late-decision conflict, expiry announcement.

### WP-6 OPERATIONAL PROTOCOL VERIFIED — evidence-source metadata added; reporting semantics made explicit

**Two refinements, both operational — the framework itself is untouched.**

**P-1 — reporting semantics stated explicitly.** My "tie-break" wording left it ambiguous whether the four outcomes were exclusive *in reality* or only *in reporting*. Now stated as the ARB put it: **multiple outcomes may be true during a work package; the record reports only the STRONGEST applicable outcome, to keep slices comparable.** With the exception spelled out: **`Failed to classify` is recorded whenever it occurs regardless of what else applies** — it is the only reopening trigger, so it must never be masked by a stronger-sounding outcome. **A reporting convention, not a framework rule.**

**P-2 — evidence source added to the template.** Every outcome now names *how* it was obtained: RED test · GREEN implementation · architectural review · static analysis · integration verification · operational/runtime observation. **Metadata about the record, not a ninth dimension.**

**Why it earns its place, and it is not cosmetic:** the field only becomes interesting *in aggregate*. If after several slices every "Exposed" traces to **architectural review** and none to a gate, that indicts the **gates**, not the framework. **WP-6 already shows exactly that pattern — all seven findings (N-5, O-1, O-4, O-5, S-1..S-3) came from review passes; none from a failing gate.** One slice cannot support that conclusion, which is precisely why the field exists: so several can. *(Note the resonance with AD-009/AD-010 — guard coverage gaps found earlier by the same route.)*

**Four verification phases passed:** completeness (all five records, no framework concept added) · traceability (evidence source present) · reporting semantics (documented as a convention) · continuity (record in the work package · discoverable via the `MEMORY.md` pointer · **no new governance document**).

> **The operational protocol is verified. The framework has three distinct layers: (1) frozen architectural concepts, (2) frozen evidence collection process, (3) evolving operational evidence. Future confidence will come from applying the protocol unchanged across independent work packages.**

**Framework and protocol work is now COMPLETE.** Attention returns to delivery; the only outstanding gate on WP-6 is the ARB re-scope ruling.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN: MAD-aware horizon, late-decision conflict, expiry announcement.

### FINAL OPERATIONAL READINESS — I overreached on the evidence and it is corrected; meta-engineering CLOSES

**The overreach was mine, and the pattern matters more than the instance.** In the protocol-verification entry above I wrote that WP-6's findings *"indict the gates"* — a **conclusion drawn from one slice**, in the same paragraph where I had written that one slice cannot support a conclusion. Corrected into the three levels the protocol exists to enforce:

- **Observed evidence:** all seven findings (N-5 · O-1 · O-4 · O-5 · S-1..S-3) were identified by review passes; none by a failing gate.
- **Hypothesis (not a conclusion):** *WP-6 suggests review activities identified findings that automated gates did not.* **Whether this reflects a systematic gap in gate coverage requires evidence from multiple independent work packages.**
- **Conclusion:** none available. One slice establishes a pattern to watch, never a systemic defect. *(A second data point may exist in AD-009/AD-010, found by the same route — two is still not several.)*

**Why this correction matters beyond the wording:** a protocol that overreaches on its **first record** teaches future readers that its records can be discounted. The protocol's credibility is its only asset.

**Evidence-Source values refined** at ARB direction — **Architectural Review · Code Review · RED Test · GREEN Verification · Static Analysis · Integration Verification · Runtime Observation.** Rationale: *not every review exercises the framework.* Separating **Architectural Review** from **Code Review** lets future aggregate analysis distinguish *"the framework found it"* from *"someone reading the diff found it"* — resolution that matters precisely for the hypothesis above. Still **metadata**, still outside the framework and the taxonomy; **no dimension added.**

**Readiness confirmed:** framework development complete · protocol development complete · evidence discipline verified · Evidence Source remains operational metadata · **WP-7 needs no framework or protocol work, only an operational record** · operational records are the sole source of framework evolution.

> **The framework and its operational protocol are ready for long-term use. Future evidence will come from operational application across independent work packages. Next milestone: ARB re-scope ruling → GREEN → complete WP-6 → record the first operational outcome → apply the unchanged framework to WP-7.**

**🔒 META-ENGINEERING CLOSES HERE.** The remaining uncertainty in both artifacts can only be reduced by **use**. Attention returns to software delivery.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling** → GREEN: MAD-aware horizon · late-decision conflict · expiry announcement. Then WP-6 closure with its first operational record.

### EVIDENTIAL NORMALIZATION — framework/protocol WORKSTREAM CLOSED

**One overstatement remained, and I found it by scanning rather than recalling.** Grepping the conclusion-verbs (proves, demonstrates, indicts, establishes) returned three hits: two were quoted before-text inside correction records (legitimate — a correction must quote what it corrects) and **one was live prose of mine**: *"one slice **establishes** a pattern to watch"* became *"**surfaces**"*.

**Two wording corrections, both accepted:**

- **"Hypothesis" to "Operational question."** The framework guides **engineering decisions**; it does not test scientific propositions. *"Does this pattern persist across independent work packages?"* is answerable by engineering practice — *"hypothesis"* borrowed an epistemology the framework does not use.
- **"Evidence Source answers how the framework earned its result" to "metadata identifying where the supporting evidence originated."** The framework has **no agency**: it classified, exposed, or failed to classify. Attributing achievement to an instrument invites treating its output as **authority** — the exact opposite of the evidence-to-recommendation-to-authority separation this program spent the day defending.

**DDD justification worth keeping:** ubiquitous language applies to the **process vocabulary** too. *"Hypothesis"* and *"earned"* imported a scientific and an agentive frame into a vocabulary otherwise strictly about **responsibility and evidence** — the same category discipline that put process exceptions in the Application layer (N-5) and kept governance outside the framework (T-1). **A framework whose own language drifts cannot be trusted to police drift elsewhere.**

**The three epistemic levels now stand apart:** Observed evidence (WP-6 only), Operational question, cross-slice validation required. No statement combines two.

> **The operational protocol satisfies the project's evidence discipline. The framework/protocol WORKSTREAM IS CLOSED.** Every remaining improvement would be about expression, not architecture.

**Delivery resumes.** Architecture stable, governance stable, protocol stable. The only remaining source of meaningful evidence is operational use across independent work packages.

## Next Steps
**ARB re-scope ruling**, then GREEN (MAD-aware horizon, late-decision conflict, expiry announcement), then WP-6 closure with its first operational record, then WP-7 under the unchanged framework.
# Session 2026-07-31

*Continues `.claude/sessions/2026-07-30.md` (PKS Phase II governance programme). The preceding log's final entries — the ER-F1/ER-F2 disposition and Discipline Amendment 4 — belong to THIS day; see the chronology correction below.*

## Summary

Disposed ER-F1/ER-F2 (accept both, remedies applied), recorded Discipline Amendment 4, corrected a chronology error in my own audit records, and recorded the Authority's refinements: retention as the observability condition for lawful maintenance, the five governance domains with their orthogonality precision, the full admission chain, and the polished-history principle.

## ⚠️ Chronology correction — self-inflicted, in acts about chronology

**The ER-F1/ER-F2 disposition, Discipline Amendment 4 and the Execution Record's maintenance note were all stamped `2026-07-30`. They were executed on `2026-07-31`.** **Corrected in all four artifacts** (ER-1 review §14 + its currency row + traceability · the Discipline's Amendment 4 heading · the Execution Record's maintained-header note).

***Recorded plainly rather than quietly fixed: a mis-dated stamp in an execution ledger is the same defect class as ER-F1 — a claim about time that does not match the time — and it was committed by the act that repaired ER-F1. The correction is metadata; no substance moved.***

## Completed

- **ER-F1 + ER-F2 — ACCEPTED, remedies APPLIED** (see 2026-07-30 log for the full record). Classified **MAINTENANCE**; zero segment bodies touched; original header text retained verbatim; §3/§8 preserved; nothing renumbered.
- **Self-application:** ER-1's own header annotated (frozen artifact → annotation), qualifier adjacent to the lapsed claim.
- **Discipline Amendment 4** — recognition ≠ agreement ≠ adoption, GOVERNED on five cross-context data.

## Decisions

**1. ⭐ RETENTION is the OBSERVABILITY CONDITION for lawful maintenance** *(Authority formulation, stronger than mine and adopted in its place)*:
> **Without retention of the superseded text, maintenance is OBSERVATIONALLY INDISTINGUISHABLE from rewriting history.**

**Four properties hold together with retention — the full test:** current state visible · **historical state immediately RECONSTRUCTIBLE** · no sealed segment changed · chronology intact. ***A maintainer who cannot be distinguished from a rewriter has not earned the distinction, however honest the intent. Retention makes the claim checkable by a reader who does not trust the maintainer.***

**2. THE FIVE GOVERNANCE DOMAINS recorded** (knowledge · execution · archival · methodology · meta-governance) — ***domains, NOT review families, NOT contracts, NOT emphases: different APPLICATIONS of one discipline, not expansions of the taxonomy.***

**⚠️ Orthogonality precision recorded because the collision is real:** there are **five review families** and **five governance domains**, and ***the coincidence of count is ACCIDENTAL — the axes are orthogonal.*** A family classifies **what a review EXAMINES**; a domain classifies **what governance ACTIVITY is performed**. ***"Execution" appears in both and means different things.*** **ER-1 is the proof: a PRESERVATION-family review operating in the ARCHIVAL and EXECUTION domains at once.**

**3. Amendment 4 extended with the FULL ADMISSION CHAIN:** Recognition → Agreement → Methodological admission (if applicable) → Assessment → Authority disposition → Adoption.
> **AGREEMENT DOES NOT POSSESS LEGISLATIVE FORCE** — regardless of source (reviewer, architect, or Authority). *Otherwise governance collapses into preference.*

***PMR-8 is the load-bearing evidence precisely because immediate adoption was the easiest path: a gate is only demonstrable when passing through it costs the party who could have skipped it.***

**4. A POLISHED HISTORY CONCEALS THE DECISION POINT** *(Authority)* — an auditable history shows future readers **both** the inconsistency **and** the reasoning that resolved it. ***The value of the record is not that it is clean but that a later reader can find where judgment was exercised and check it.***

## Problems

**The Authority identified its own contradictory instruction and confirmed the second statement (the evidential boundary) was correct while the first overstepped it.** *Recorded because it validates the handling: the ambiguity was declared rather than silently resolved, and the resolution supplied the missing evidence inside the same act.*
