## Final wording refinement · the four-instrument separation recorded (2026-07-31)

### ⚠️ Causal claim made PROPORTIONAL
**Superseded:** ~~*"collapsing them IS how a correct decision produces a non-compliant implementation"*~~ — *stated as a causal law from three examples.*
**Operative (Authority formulation):** ***these cases show how collapsing decision and implementation design CAN ALLOW a correct decision to produce a non-compliant implementation*** — **three examples support the observation without establishing a universal causal law.**

### ⭐ THE FOUR-INSTRUMENT SEPARATION recorded (Integrity Model)
| Instrument | Its act |
|---|---|
| **Review** | establishes **EVIDENCE** |
| **Authority** | determines **WHAT** should change |
| **Change-control commission** | derives a **COMPLIANT IMPLEMENTATION MECHANISM** within existing governance constraints |
| **Execution** | performs **ONLY** that commissioned mechanism |

***What the third instrument is for: the Authority never decided "insert a scope clause in §2" — it decided "make the contested memberships visible." Everything after that required architectural reasoning constrained by existing governance, which is why WHAT and HOW are different acts.***

**Distinguished from the programme's more tentative observations: this separation has been exercised REPEATEDLY across DIFFERENT ARTIFACT TYPES (AD-1 · C4-1 · the CDR · SDM v1.1 · M7) — a stronger evidence base than a pattern seen in one kind of act.**

**The AFV-F4 commission asserted *"the repository is the PKS domain"* with four roots in and `./architecture/` excluded as *"legacy brainstorming, ungoverned"*.**

2. **ES-005 declares it *"supersedes README/MEMORY as the rule homes for the folder rule"*** — a rule home already exists; a determination ignoring it would amend it by implication.
3. **`.claude/CLAUDE.md`: *"`architecture/` = **Think**"*** — a ROLE, not an exclusion. ***"Legacy during transition" ≠ "ungoverned".***
4. **≥12 top-level roots** (app · architecture · bin · bootstrap · business_analysis · claude · config · database · developer_guide · developer_issues · docs · engineering), and **`architecture/` alone holds 465 markdown files.** *The four-root framing omits ≥7 roots and calls a 465-file tree residue.*

> **Does ES-005.1 GOVERN repository scope today?**

*Tension the assessment must reconcile: **ES-005's document status is PROPOSED**, but **ES-005.1 cites "(EM-001, ARB 2026-07-10)"** — the RULE carries an ARB ruling even where the DOCUMENT does not.*

**Three outcomes: (A) ES-005.1 governs → scope is ALREADY DETERMINED and the AFV-F4 framing was simply wrong · (B) rule ARB-ruled but document unadopted → the question is ES-005's ADOPTION, a different act on a different path · (C) neither governs → only then is a fresh determination in scope.**

***The ordering is binding: a determination made before settling this would either DUPLICATE a governed rule or AMEND it silently.***

### Constraints written in
**No embedded factual assertions** (§1's facts are cited with sources and may be overturned) · **⛔ must NOT retro-ground AFV-F4** — *that would be a RATIONALE change to a discharged act, itself a governance change* · **⛔ NO cleanup authorized, and the proposed cleanup would potentially VIOLATE ES-005.1** · promoted artifacts reachable only by change control · **asymmetry to be VERIFIED, not asserted.**

**Deliverables include a statement on EVERY top-level root, so the four-root framing is not inherited silently.**

## ⛔ "Operationally enforced" CORRECTED — four evidence strengths applied (2026-07-31)

**The Authority's four-tier ladder made me test a claim I had asserted on an index row: *"ES-005.1's classification is OPERATIONALLY ENFORCED."* It compressed four distinct strengths and overstated at least two.**

| Strength | R-37 | ES-005.1 |
|---|---|---|
| **RECORDED** *(somebody wrote it)* | ✅ rulings log | ✅ in ES-005 |
| **REFERENCED** *(later artifacts rely on it)* | ✅ complements R-27 | ✅ STANDARDS_INDEX §54 assigns instruments; **OQ-ENG-002 §50 cites the "three-concern model" in a PASS row** |
| **IMPLEMENTED** *(execution follows it)* | not separately tested | ⚠️ **PARTIALLY** — used as a criterion in an EXECUTED qualification (OQ-ENG-002, 2026-07-11), **but that row checks README's entry-point clarity, NOT the folder classification; and only TWO OQ-ENG executions exist (07-10, 07-11), none since** |
| **BINDING** *(Authority adopted it)* | ✅ **adopted ARB ruling** | ⛔ **NOT DEMONSTRATED** — ES-005 PROPOSED; the three-concern text's adoption unestablished |

> ***My phrase asserted implementation and implied binding force from a single index row. Corrected: RECORDED and REFERENCED with certainty · PARTIALLY IMPLEMENTED on one dated execution · NOT DEMONSTRATED as binding.***

**⚠️ Further precision: STANDARDS_INDEX says the instruments are *"executed each OQ"* — true, and there has been NO OQ since 2026-07-11.** ***The enforcement is real but DORMANT; "executed each OQ" reads as ongoing when the last execution is three weeks old.***

### Three Authority insights recorded (Integrity Model)
**1. FOUR STRENGTHS of evidence that a rule governs** — recorded · referenced · implemented · binding. ***Increasing strengths that must not be compressed. A rule can be recorded and referenced without being implemented; implemented without being binding; and binding without any instrument having run recently.***

**2. ⭐ SCOPE vs CLASSIFICATION are DIFFERENT GOVERNANCE OBJECTS:** **R-37 governs STRUCTURE** (*what may exist*) · **ES-005.1 governs MEANING** (*what existing things ARE*). ***Same date, same migration, adjacent artifacts — and conflating them is how "the repository is the PKS domain" came to be asserted as though a structural freeze had settled a classification question.***

**3. THREE kinds of "already governed":** already **DISPOSED** (AFV-F4) · already **PARTIALLY governed** (repository scope) · already **REVIEWED but no longer reproducible** (IBC-1). ***Three different constitutional situations, all requiring the same first question: does governance already exist here?*** **NOT elevated into methodology — recorded as a characteristic of this programme, not a required stage.**

**STATE: repository-scope determination CLOSED at Outcome B with evidence strengths corrected · MCA-R2 commissioned and unexecuted · sixteen artifacts reviewed · no review open · no finding open · register 10.**

## 🏁 MCA-R2 EXECUTED — PMR-8 insufficient · PMR-9 CONFIRMED · PMR-10 CONFIRMED (2026-07-31)

**`docs/implementation/PKS_Phase_II_MCA_R2_Assessment.md`.** Two Authority wording refinements applied first: *"execution evidence shows AT LEAST ONE implemented use… does NOT establish CONTINUOUS OR GENERAL implementation"* (bounds the extent) and the five-case pattern restated as a **tendency**, not a law.

### §1 — THE CENTRAL QUESTION SETTLED FIRST, as a READING of the existing filter
> **ESCAPE means the defect LEFT THE STAGE THAT OWNED THE CHECK** — grounded in RET-1 §5.3's operative sense (*"defects later stages found"*). **A defect PREVENTED, or CAUGHT WITHIN the act that produced it, is NOT an escape.**

*Offered as interpretation, not mandate (CDR-R1 §1's boundary); PMR-8 was not applied to PMR-8.*

### ⭐ §1.1 — the procedure-taint RESIDUAL resolves as a CONSEQUENCE, not separately
| | |
|---|---|
| evidence **PRODUCED BY** the practice | ***yields counterfactuals*** — the practice was applied, so nothing escaped |
| evidence of **ESCAPE** | ***is procedure-INDEPENDENT*** — an escape is an observation of FAILURE, and failures are not products of the practice under assessment |

> ***The taint concern applies to exactly the candidates whose evidence is counterfactual and not at all to those with escapes. The residual is the same distinction seen from the other side.***

### Verdicts
- **PMR-8 — INSUFFICIENT EVIDENCE. n=0 escapes.** *Its sole datum is MCA-R1, where the step WAS performed and false rejections were PREVENTED.* ***Structurally self-defeating in the exact way PMR-1's was: PMR-1 failed because routing was "consistent"; PMR-8 fails because the step was PERFORMED. A candidate whose only datum is its own successful voluntary application cannot demonstrate escape.*** **Discharge (a DATA condition): one instance where absent applicability let a false verdict ESCAPE into a decision.**
- **PMR-9 — ✅ CONFIRMED on THREE escapes:** CON-1 (the *"new obligation"* ontology error, caught by the **Authority**) · M7-CF2 (*"strengthened"* and *"class change"*, both caught by the Authority) · IBC-1 re-certification (IBC-M4 graded *"strengthened"*, caught later by the ladder). **MC-1 EXCLUDED — self-caught within the act, not an escape.**
- **PMR-10 — ✅ CONFIRMED on TWO escapes:** the **AFV** collision (blocked an Authority recommendation across three legitimate readings) · the **MCA** collision (**published a false claim that stood three days**). ***The second is one of the strongest individual data points currently in the register: the collision was minted while the programme was actively recording that names cannot be withdrawn — knowing the cost demonstrably did not produce compliance, because the identifier check was NOT AN EXPLICIT REQUIRED STEP, allowing the collision to occur despite awareness of the underlying risk — which is precisely what a control changes.***

### ⚠️ THE REGISTER'S COUNT RE-DERIVED AGAINST THE CRITERIA
**PMR-9's recorded n=2 was INCONSISTENT WITH THE ASSESSMENT CRITERIA** — it counted MC-1 (not an escape) and omitted two escapes. ***The undercount is itself a datum for the candidate: the register recorded instances by NOTABILITY rather than by the filter's own test — which is what happens when classification precedes verification.***

### Boundaries
Nothing adopted · PMR-8 not applied to PMR-8 (the filter was READ, and all three are controls so no applicability adaptation was needed) · **counts NOT aggregated** · the filter's blind spot (*it measures only defect prevention*) **carried forward UNRESOLVED** · no new candidate; register stays at 10 · SDM v1.1 / EOP v1.1 operative.

**FOR THE CDR, not decided here:** adopt PMR-9? · adopt PMR-10? · does PMR-8's narrower value warrant adoption despite failing an escape-based filter? · **should the filter's blind spot itself be revisited?**

## MCA-R2 — four Authority wording refinements applied (2026-07-31)

**1. Superlative bounded:** ~~*"the strongest single datum in the register"*~~ → ***"one of the strongest individual data points currently in the register."*** *Ranking evidence is harder than identifying it, and the programme has no explicit weighting framework — so a superlative asserted a comparison it could not perform.*

**2. Interpretive phrase replaced with observable evidence:** ~~*"the check was a HABIT rather than a STEP"*~~ → ***"the identifier check was NOT AN EXPLICIT REQUIRED STEP, allowing the collision to occur despite awareness of the underlying risk."***

**3. Register characterization corrected:** ~~*"the register's n=2 was WRONG IN COMPOSITION and LOW"*~~ → ***"the register's counts were INCONSISTENT WITH THE ASSESSMENT CRITERIA."*** ***The EVIDENCE did not change; its CLASSIFICATION against the filter did.*** *This is governance improving its own bookkeeping, not revising an evidence base.*

**4. PMR-8's outcome stated precisely:**
> ⚠️ **This does NOT show that PMR-8 lacks value. It shows that THE CURRENT ADMISSION CRITERION — escape evidence — HAS NOT BEEN SATISFIED.**

***The candidate may still have preventive value; it has simply not met the programme's current threshold for admission. Those are different findings, and only the second is within an assessment's authority to make.***

**All three sets of counts, verdicts and evidence are unchanged by these refinements — only the characterizations were tightened.**

### PMR-9 — ✅ ADOPT, verbatim
> **Where a finding's CLASS depends on what a governing source actually says, the source shall be verified before the finding is classified — not merely before it is asserted.**

***Why the evidence is HETEROGENEOUS rather than REPETITIVE: the three escapes occurred at different times, on different artifacts, and produced DIFFERENT ERROR TYPES — an ontology error (CON-1), a severity error (M7-CF2), a grading error (IBC-M4). That is more convincing than three repetitions of one mistake, because a single recurring slip could be idiosyncratic while three distinct failure shapes indicate a MISSING STEP.***

### PMR-10 — ✅ ADOPT, verbatim
> **Before an identifier or acronym is minted, the corpus shall be searched for existing use of that token. Where the token is taken, a distinct one is chosen.**

**⚠️ Prospective scope matters more here than for PMR-9:** ***the two existing collisions CANNOT be cured by this rule — identifier stability forbids renaming, so they are bounded, never erased. Adoption prevents the THIRD collision; it does not repair the first two.*** **Renaming `AFV-1` / `MCA-R1` remains FORBIDDEN; no retroactive collision audit is authorized; the AFV normalization stays on the backlog by ALIAS only.**

**⚠️ Structural difficulty recorded rather than glossed: PMR-8's discharge requires a governance FAILURE the programme is actively trying to prevent.** *The condition is satisfiable only by the control's absence causing harm — hardest to obtain for candidates already practised voluntarily. Whether that is a correct property of the filter or a defect in it is the blind-spot question.*

## ES-005-ADOPT-1 — two refinements applied · two principles recorded (2026-07-31)

### 1. Implementation status no longer attributed UNIFORMLY
**Superseded:** ~~*"already recorded, referenced and implemented at least once"*~~
**Operative:** ***"already recorded and referenced, with evidence of at least one implementation use WHERE SEPARATELY DEMONSTRATED."***
*Implementation evidence was demonstrated for the **three-concern classification only** — the decision must not carry it across to the folder rule or the placement litmus without their own evidence.* **My phrasing would have attributed implementation to all three ARB-cited rules on evidence for one.**

### 2. The eight-roots concern stated more precisely
**Superseded:** ~~*"adoption would FREEZE that gap into a governed rule"*~~
**Operative:** ***"adopting a repository-classification rule without explicitly stating the status of the remaining top-level roots risks unintentionally implying that the governed classification is EXHAUSTIVE."***
***The narrower claim is the stronger one: the risk is not that the roots become frozen but that READERS MAY REASONABLY INFER COMPLETENESS where none was decided.***

### ⭐ 3. ADOPTION CHANGES BINDING STATUS, NOT CONTENT (Integrity Model — governance vocabulary)
| Act | Changes |
|---|---|
| **EDITING** a rule | its **content** *(own authority required; forbidden during adoption)* |
| **ADOPTING** a rule | its **binding status** — hence its **enforceability**, not current practice |
| **IMPLEMENTING** a rule | whether **execution follows** it |

***A rule can be implemented before it is adopted, and adopted without any change in behaviour. Adoption is about ENFORCEABILITY.***

### ⭐ 4. THE GOVERNANCE UNIT SHOULD MATCH THE AUTHORITY UNIT (Integrity Model)
**Six instances: findings → the individual PROPOSITION (CON-F2→M7-F1) · PMRs → the individual CANDIDATE (PMR-7 split) · ES-005 → the individual RULE, not the document · M7 → the individual CHANGE ITEM (C-M7-01) · SDM/EOP → the BASELINE ISSUANCE · CDR → the individual DISPOSITION.**

> ***Avoid bundling decisions whose EVIDENCE, AUTHORITY or MATURITY differ. Where they differ and are bundled, the strongest element carries the weakest — which is the composite-confidence defect (PMR-2) operating on GOVERNANCE OBJECTS rather than on CLAIMS.***

**Held as an observed architectural direction, not admitted as a rule.**

### Noted on the two rules exercised
**The Authority's framing adopted: PMR-10's value is not *"no collision occurred"* but that ***the collision check was EXPLICITLY PERFORMED before minting*** — an explicit governance step rather than an implicit habit. Likewise PMR-9's value is not that the citations agreed but that ***classification followed VERIFICATION rather than RECOLLECTION.***

## Bundling MECHANISM stated · ES-005-ADOPT-1 readiness confirmed but NOT self-authorized (2026-07-31)

### The bundling mechanism, explained rather than described
**Superseded, retained:** ~~*"the strongest element carries the weakest"*~~ — *described the OUTCOME without the mechanism, and asserted that every bundle fails.*
**Operative (Authority formulation):**
> **When governance objects with different evidence, authority, or maturity are bundled into a single decision, THE BUNDLE SHARES ONE AUTHORITY BOUNDARY WHILE ITS EVIDENCE REMAINS UNEVEN. That can allow weaker elements to acquire governance status they have not independently justified.**

***Ties directly to the composite-confidence reasoning (PMR-2) without assuming inevitability: bundling CAN allow unjustified elevation, and whether it does depends on the bundle.***

### ⚠️ READINESS IS NOT AUTHORIZATION — the commission is NOT executed
**The Authority's message concludes that ES-005-ADOPT-1 *"is now ready for execution"* and lists the properties supporting that. It does not instruct execution.**

> ***Readiness is a DERIVED STATE, not a DECISION — the same distinction the promotion chain already carries ("Promotion Ready is a derived state, not a decision"; verified-ready ≠ promoted). Executing on a readiness finding would collapse the two.***

**ES-005-ADOPT-1 therefore stands COMMISSIONED, REFINED, and READY — awaiting an explicit instruction to execute.**

**The readiness properties the Authority verified, recorded:** evidence verified before recommendation · per-rule rather than wholesale adoption · no embedded assumptions about binding status · no propagation of implementation evidence · no unintended expansion of repository classification · clear separation between content, adoption and implementation.

**STATE: ES-005-ADOPT-1 COMMISSIONED and READY, NOT EXECUTED · SDM v1.2 / EOP v1.2 operative · sixteen artifacts reviewed · no review open · no finding open · register 10.**

**`docs/implementation/PKS_ES005_Adoption_Decision.md`. Unit of decision = THE RULE, not the document.**

### ⛔ §1 — A COUNT CORRECTION MADE BEFORE DECIDING
**The commission said *"eight unclassified roots."* Wrong — it came from a TRUNCATED listing.**
**24 top-level directories · 6 classified by ES-005.1 (`docs` · `architecture` · `app` · `tests` · `engineering` · `.claude`) · **18 UNCLASSIFIED**.**

> ***The non-exhaustiveness concern is more than TWICE as large as the commission stated — and PMR-9, governed since this morning, is what caught it: the count was re-derived from source rather than carried forward from recollection.***

**Two facts also settled:** `tests/` **exists** (a prior concern dissolves) · **`.claude/` and `claude/` BOTH exist**, and only the dotted runtime mount is classified.
**And the gap's shape recorded so it need not be re-derived: **inspection indicates many unclassified roots are FRAMEWORK OR INFRASTRUCTURE directories** — ***no governing act establishes whether their omission from ES-005.1 was intentional*** *(observable facts separated from inferred intent).*

### The four decisions
- **ES-005.1 — ✅ ADOPT, with a NON-EXHAUSTIVENESS RIDER forming part of the adoption:** *the classification is NOT exhaustive — 6 of 24 roots; the remaining 18 are unclassified and this adoption classifies none of them.* Rule ARB-ruled · recorded · referenced · **one verified implementation use** (OQ-ENG-002 §50) · **now BINDING**.
- **ES-005.2 — ✅ ADOPT.** ARB-ruled; implementation **INDEX-CLAIMED but no instance separately verified** (the row covers .1 and .2 jointly; only .1's use was verified). **R-37 relationship determined: COMPLEMENTARY, and R-37 STRICTER at the top level** — *ES-005.2 governs WHEN a directory may be created (any level); R-37 FREEZES top-level folders. Neither redundant; at the top level R-37 governs. R-37 unamended.*
- **ES-005.3 — ✅ ADOPT** despite **"None" instruments** — ***adoption changes BINDING STATUS rather than implementation; a rule may be binding and unenforced by machine, and ES-005.3's enforcement is by judgment at EP-02 review as the index records.***
- **ES-005.4 — ⏸ DEFER.** Its own text: ***"candidate, ARB to confirm scope."*** **Adoption is UNAVAILABLE, not merely inadvisable — a rule whose scope is unconfirmed cannot be made binding without deciding the scope by implication.** Discharge is a **DECISION condition** — the register's only one. ***The CLEAREST JUSTIFICATION for per-rule adoption emerged here: as a document, this rule would have become binding through BUNDLING with its scope decided by nobody.*** *(The commission also resolved ES-005.2's relationship to R-37, ES-005.3's adoption despite implementation differences, and the non-exhaustiveness rider — those are genuine outcomes too.)*

### Remaining matters
**STANDARDS_INDEX's PROPOSED status does not gate the adoptions** (ES-005 is canonical; the index registers pointers) — *but the navigation layer remains unadopted.* **Supersession EFFECTED for ES-005.2 and ES-005.3 only; README remains the entry-point summary.** **Adoption changes ENFORCEABILITY, not practice — no artifact requires amendment.**

### NOT decided
the 18 unclassified roots · **ES-005 as a document (remains PROPOSED — it still hosts an undecided candidate)** · STANDARDS_INDEX's adoption · R-37 · ES-005.4's scope · any file move or reorganization · Outcome B · certification state.

NOT elevated into a methodology stage.*

## 🏁 SDM-EXT-1 COMMISSIONED — administrative extraction/consolidation (2026-07-31) · NOT EXECUTABLE until two matters are settled

**`docs/implementation/PKS_SDM_EXT_1_Extraction_Commission.md`.** Label collision-checked under **PMR-10 (GOVERNED)**. Authorizing act quoted verbatim from **CDR §4.2**.

### ⛔ RESERVED MATTER ONE — the authorization names **v1**; the baseline is **v1.2**
**CDR §4.2 authorized consolidating *"the frozen content into single SDM-v1/EOP-v1 documents."*** Since then v1.1 added three adoptions and v1.2 added two.
> **Does an authorization granted for v1's content extend to a baseline it did not contemplate?**
**Reading A:** it authorizes an ACT TYPE (administrative, zero new design content) and follows the current baseline. **Reading B:** it named **v1**, and the five later adoptions need their own authorization.
***NOT decided. Executing an extraction of v1.2 under a v1 authorization would act on an unverified premise — the pattern the AFV-F4 and repository-scope commissions were both built to prevent.***

### ⛔ RESERVED MATTER TWO — extraction CONVERTS the baseline's architecture
> **Extraction is not merely a documentation task: it converts **Model B** (reference-defined — *one authoritative wording per governed statement, no copy that can drift*) to **Model A** (materialized), REINTRODUCING the defect class Model B eliminates.**

**Three sources bear, and they do not agree:**
- **Assembly Fidelity Rule — GOVERNED (v1.1):** an extraction document **IS an assembly artifact**, so the rule binds it directly. ***And PMR-5's own evidence was the eleven-site cascade — restatement creates one defect site PER SITE, so extraction creates a second site for EVERY governed statement.***
- **ES-005.4 "Never a Copy" — DEFERRED hours ago:** *"it never duplicates them. One rule → one home; everything else points."* ***Were it binding it would arguably FORBID this act. It is deferred, so it does not bind — but the Authority should decide knowingly, not by default.***
- **ISV-1's finding:** extraction would create the derived text a reference-defined baseline lacks — **but the Authority has ruled consolidation must NOT be undertaken to enable a verification.** ***So that is a CONSEQUENCE, never a justification.***

### §3 — the justification must stand alone
**CDR §4.2 itself records that *"the record is complete and citable as it stands."*** ***So extraction improves NAVIGATION, not COMPLETENESS — and a navigation gain must be weighed against a reintroduced drift-risk class.***

### §4 — if authorized, four binding constraints
zero new design content · **every governed statement VERBATIM, with verification before any verbatim claim** (mechanical extraction + post-hoc diff, per the v1.2 precedent) · forward-only supersession · **⚠️ the DRIFT QUESTION answered explicitly — *which artifact governs on conflict? Leaving it unstated creates two homes for one rule, the exact condition ES-005.4 names.***

### §6 — NO recommendation offered
***Four materially different considerations: authorized by CDR §4.2 · arguably forbidden by a rule deferred hours ago · binding an assembly artifact under a governed fidelity rule · converting the baseline's architecture. Compressing them would repeat the bundling error the ES-005 adoption was just structured to avoid.***

> **The baseline ALREADY EXISTS as Model B, so Model B is not a proposal requiring justification — Model A is.** ***The question is not "can we extract?" but "why should the architecture STOP being reference-defined?"***

**COMPLETENESS** ⛔ — *CDR §4.2 itself records the record is "complete and citable as it stands"; if completeness already exists, extraction cannot be justified by it.* **ENABLING VERIFICATION** ⛔ — *ruled out by the Authority; ISV-1's derived-text point is a CONSEQUENCE, never a reason.*

### ⭐ §4 — ES-005.4 usable as authority in NEITHER direction
> ***A deferred rule is not a weak rule pointing one way. It is SILENT. Citing it in either direction would grant governance force to something the Authority expressly declined to make binding.***

*It would have aligned with retaining Model B — and is unavailable to that argument, having been deferred hours earlier.* **Recorded in the Integrity Model as a general principle.**

### §5 — FIVE acts of accumulated DIRECTION, recorded as EVIDENCE, not a verdict
verbatim adoption · the GOVERNED Assembly Fidelity Rule · PMR-9 · per-rule governance (PMR-7 split, ES-005 per-rule) · the eleven-site KC-13 cascade — **all reduce duplicated text, all decided independently of this question.**
> ***Five acts pointing one way is a FACT about the programme's history, not an argument that the direction is correct. The Authority may decide against it on grounds none of those acts considered.***

### §6 — consequences of each outcome
**Model B retained → SDM-EXT-1 is RETIRED, not deferred: its premise fails.** *ES-005.4's scope confirmation becomes more consequential.*
**Model A adopted → SDM-EXT-1 becomes executable, and its v1-vs-v1.2 question must STILL be answered; the four constraints bind, and the Assembly Fidelity Rule binds the extraction PERMANENTLY, not once.**

### No recommendation offered
***The Authority's framing already supplies the burden, the unavailable justifications, and the deferred-rule silence. A recommendation would add nothing — and would risk supplying it as authority.***

## BRM-1 — burden test made EXPLICIT · two principles recorded (2026-07-31)

### The burden, extended from WHERE it sits to WHAT it is
**Superseded:** ~~*"the burden is on the change"*~~ — *stated where the burden sits without stating what it is.*
**Operative (Authority formulation):**
> **THE BURDEN IS ON THE PROPOSED CHANGE to demonstrate benefits that JUSTIFY INTRODUCING A NEW GOVERNANCE RISK NOT PRESENT IN THE CURRENT ARCHITECTURE.**

**Balancing test explicit: not *"is extraction useful?"* but ***"are the benefits sufficient to justify reintroducing duplicated-governance risk?"***

**⚠️ And what it is NOT: a presumption that Model B is SUPERIOR.** ***It is the ordinary architectural principle that the existing architecture does not have to justify its own existence every time a change is proposed. The proposed change must justify itself.***

### ⭐ Two principles recorded (Integrity Model)
**1. THE BURDEN OF A PROPOSED ARCHITECTURAL CHANGE** — as above, with the not-a-presumption qualifier attached.
**2. HISTORICAL CONSISTENCY IS EVIDENCE OF DIRECTION, NOT AN ARGUMENT FOR CONTINUING IT** — ***otherwise governance becomes PATH-DEPENDENT simply because previous commissions happened to move one way.*** *Demonstrated at BRM-1 §5: five acts reducing duplicated text recorded as evidence a direction had emerged, expressly not as an argument that it should continue.*

### The pattern the Authority named, recorded
**Five commissions have now discovered that the requested act was the wrong question:** AFV-F4 *(premise already discharged)* · repository scope *(an adoption question, not a fresh determination)* · M7-CF2 *(ontology changed → a new finding, not the old one carried)* · ES-005 *(per-rule, not document adoption)* · **SDM-EXT-1 *(an architectural fork, not an editorial act)***.

***In each case the commission's value was not answering its question but establishing that a prior question governed it.***

### Holding at BRM-1
**The Authority's sequence names *"Execute BRM-1"* as step 1, framed advisorily (*"my sequence would be"*, *"if this were my programme"*).** **BRM-1's execution IS the architectural decision — Model A or Model B — which is an Authority act.** ***Holding for the same reason the Authority endorsed at ES-005-ADOPT-1: a recommended sequence is not an authorization, and readiness is a derived state.*** **Ready on the word.**

## 🏁 BRM-1 DECIDED — RETAIN MODEL B · SDM-EXT-1 RETIRED (2026-07-31)

**`docs/implementation/PKS_BRM_1_Decision.md`.** Governing standard applied: ***do the benefits of materialization justify introducing a duplicated-governance risk the current architecture does not have?***

### Five grounds (Authority's, as given)
existing architecture is **complete and citable** · **no demonstrated deficiency** requires abandoning the model · materialization **introduces a governance risk absent today** · the proposed benefits **have not been shown to outweigh** it · ***future tooling or presentation artifacts can improve usability WITHOUT changing the constitutional representation model.***

### ⭐ §1.1 — the rationale rests on the BURDEN TEST, not on the programme's direction
**The commission's §5 recorded five acts all reducing duplicated text, flagged expressly as *evidence of direction, not an argument for continuing it*.**
> ***The Authority's rationale cites NONE of them. Every ground is a direct application of the burden test — so the decision SURVIVES the path-dependence caution the commission itself raised. Model B is retained because the change FAILED ITS BURDEN, not because the programme had been moving that way.***

### ⭐ §2 — the CARVE-OUT, part of the decision
> **PRESENTATION is not CONSTITUTIONAL REPRESENTATION.**

**Permitted:** tooling · indexes · navigation aids · generated views · onboarding guides. **Forbidden:** *a materialized document carrying governed statements as a SECOND AUTHORITATIVE HOME.*
***The decision rejects a change to WHERE AUTHORITY LIVES, not to how the baseline is READ — so usability objections are answerable without reopening the architecture.***

### §3 — SDM-EXT-1 RETIRED, not deferred
> ***A deferral holds an act open pending EVIDENCE. No evidence short of reversing BRM-1 would revive this one — and reversing an architectural decision is a NEW ACT, not the discharge of a deferral.***

**Both its reserved matters are MOOT** (the v1-vs-v1.2 authorization question and the Model-conversion question both presupposed extraction). **CDR §4.2's authorization is UNCHANGED and now UNEXERCISED — not revoked.**

### ES-005.4 played NO part
*A deferred rule is silent — it would have aligned with Model B and was unavailable to the argument.*

### NOT decided
CDR §4.2's authorization (**not revoked**; a future Authority may revisit BRM-1) · ES-005.4's scope · STANDARDS_INDEX's adoption · the 18 unclassified roots · any tooling work (**permitted by §2, not authorized by it**) · certification state.

## ⭐ Two principles recorded (Integrity Model)
**1. PRESENTATION is not CONSTITUTIONAL REPRESENTATION** — with the permitted/forbidden split.
**2. RETIRED is not DEFERRED** — ***where no evidence short of reversing a decision would revive an act, it is RETIRED; a deferred act implies a route back that this one does not have.***

**STATE: BRM-1 DECIDED (Model B retained) · SDM-EXT-1 RETIRED · CLASS A REDUCES TO TWO: ES-005.4 scope confirmation · STANDARDS_INDEX adoption. On their completion the Authority's recommendation stands: declare the PKS Phase II Governance Baseline COMPLETE. SDM v1.2 / EOP v1.2 operative · no review open · no finding open · register 10.**

## Completion DEFINED · the seventh governance layer recorded (2026-07-31)

### ⭐ WHAT COMPLETION MEANS (Authority definition)
> **Completion does NOT mean "every possible question has been answered." It means EVERY QUESTION NECESSARY FOR THIS BASELINE HAS BEEN CONSTITUTIONALLY RESOLVED, ADOPTED, DEFERRED, OR RETIRED.**

**Four lawful terminal states:** **RESOLVED** *(repository scope → Outcome B)* · **ADOPTED** *(PMR-9 · PMR-10 · ES-005.1/.2/.3)* · **DEFERRED** *(PMR-8 · ES-005.4 · PMR-2 · PMR-3)* · **RETIRED** *(SDM-EXT-1 · CON-F2)*.

***A baseline whose open questions are all in one of these four states is COMPLETE. A baseline pursuing exhaustiveness is not more complete — it is merely unfinished in a different way.***

### ⭐ THE SEVEN GOVERNANCE RESPONSIBILITIES, fully separated
Review establishes **evidence** · Assessment **classifies** it · **Readiness** is a derived state, never an authority act · Authority decides **what changes** · Change control derives a **compliant implementation** · Execution performs **only the commissioned mechanism** · **Architectural decision determines the enduring representation model** *(BRM-1 established this layer)*.

***The seventh is the newest and highest-order: SDM-EXT-1 could not proceed because an ARCHITECTURAL DECISION governed it, and no other layer could supply one.***

### Class A — what each remaining item needs
| Item | What it needs |
|---|---|
| **ES-005.4 scope confirmation** | ***An ARB scope determination — substantive Authority content I cannot infer.*** Its own text says *"ARB to confirm scope"*, and the discharge is a DECISION condition, not a data condition |
| **STANDARDS_INDEX adoption** | **An evidence assessment I CAN prepare** *(is the index mature enough to adopt, and does its PROPOSED status gate anything?)* — **followed by an Authority decision on the conditional the Authority itself set: *"if the evidence supports it"*** |

**Then: declare the PKS Phase II Governance Baseline COMPLETE, with Classes B and C carried into the next cycle.**

**STATE: BRM-1 decided · SDM-EXT-1 retired · Class A = two items · SDM v1.2 / EOP v1.2 operative · sixteen artifacts reviewed · no review open · no finding open · register 10.**

## 🏁 SIA-1 — STANDARDS_INDEX adoption EVIDENCE ASSESSMENT (2026-07-31) · nothing decided

**`docs/implementation/PKS_SIA_1_Standards_Index_Adoption_Assessment.md`.** Label collision-checked under PMR-10. **An ASSESSMENT: it classifies evidence and decides nothing.**

### Six facts by direct read
⛔ **ALL SIX ES documents are PROPOSED** (ES-001..ES-006) · the index is PROPOSED · ⛔ **its Decision Authority Matrix CONTAINS CANDIDATE ENTRIES** (ES-004.2 *"Candidate"*; ES-005.4 *"(candidate)"*) · **it HOSTS no rules, it REGISTERS pointers** (*"copying it would create the duplication"*) · **its PROPOSED status gates nothing for the rules** (determined at ES-005-ADOPT-1) · it carries a **stopping rule** on creating new ES standards.

### ⛔ THE STRUCTURAL OBSTACLE — the ES-005 problem ONE LEVEL UP
> **Adopting the index wholesale would adopt a matrix that CLASSIFIES CANDIDATES — including ES-005.4, deferred THIS MORNING precisely to avoid making it binding by bundling.**

***At ES-005 the bundle was a document containing a candidate rule; here it is an index containing candidate classifications. And the index points to SIX PROPOSED documents — adoption would make the NAVIGATION binding while the DESTINATIONS remain unadopted.***

### §3 — what adoption would actually CHANGE
Applying the governed distinction *adoption changes binding status, not content*: it would bind **pointers that already point to self-declaring canonical homes**, plus **a matrix with undecided entries**.
> ***It is difficult to identify a currently disputed question that adoption would settle.***

### ⚠️ §4 — a FOURTH option the commission did not contemplate
**A** adopt wholesale ⛔ *blocked by §2* · **B** adopt per-element *(mirrors ES-005's pattern)* · **C** defer until targets are adopted · **D ⭐ NO ADOPTION ACT IS REQUIRED** — *the index registers pointers to self-declaring canonical homes, its PROPOSED status already gates nothing, and no disputed question would be settled.*
***Option D is the repository-scope pattern recurring: the commissioned act may not be the required one. Recorded as AVAILABLE, expressly NOT recommended.***

### §5 — the asymmetry to carry into the decision
**Maturity is NOT the obstacle** — the index is coherent, self-consistent, and observes its own no-duplication convention.
> ***The index's PROPOSED status is IRRELEVANT to what it POINTS AT, and DECISIVE for what it HOSTS — its matrix and stopping rule are index-hosted, not registered elsewhere, so their status IS the index's status.***

### Refinement applied first (Authority)
~~*"no evidence I can gather would settle it"*~~ → ***"NO EVIDENCE ALONE can settle it"*** — recorded in the Integrity Model: **evidence can supply the available interpretations, their consequences and their compatibility with existing rules; it cannot supply which interpretation the Authority intends.** *My weaker claim implied evidence is useless where it is merely insufficient.*

**`PKS_SIA_1_Decision.md`.** Standard applied: **the BRM-1 burden test.** Decisive finding: ***"it is difficult to identify a currently disputed question that adoption would settle."***

## ⛔ TWO CORRECTIONS to the completion declaration (Authority, 2026-07-31)

### 1. The "fifth terminal state" DEMOTED to an observation (n=1)
**I promoted *"no present constitutional act is required"* into the general governance ontology on ONE case.**

**Corrected:** ***SIA-1 reached that outcome, and it is none of the four states. ONE clear case is enough to USE the disposition; it is NOT enough to elevate it into the ontology. Whether it belongs there must be evaluated separately if similar cases recur.***

> ***This is the same premature-promotion error the programme resisted for the constraint-derivation stage, the "already governed" heuristic, the adjacent-level-collapse model and the escape-filter blind spot — each began as an observation before becoming a candidate. This one was granted ontology status directly.***

**⚠️ And it happened AT THE MOMENT OF DECLARING COMPLETION — when the pull toward a tidy ontology is strongest. Recorded in the Integrity Model with that circumstance attached, because the timing is the diagnostic.**

### 2. The stabilization claim SCOPED
**Superseded:** ~~*"a sequence of commissions producing 'no further act is justified' rather than new constitutional defects is evidence that the baseline has stabilized"*~~ — *read as a universal law about governance programmes.*
**Operative (Authority formulation):**
> ***WITHIN THE SCOPE OF THIS GOVERNANCE PROGRAMME, repeated commissions concluding that no additional constitutional act is justified are evidence that the baseline has STABILIZED WITH RESPECT TO THE QUESTIONS BEING EXAMINED.***

**Applied in the declaration, MEMORY.md and CONTEXT.md.**

### The Authority's change-taxonomy, recorded as the programme's largest achievement
| Change | Question |
|---|---|
| **Evidence** | Is the support stronger? |
| **Status** | Has the confidence changed? |
| **Rationale** | Has the reasoning changed? |
| **Ontology** | Has the governing proposition changed? |
| **Authority** | Does a new constitutional act exist? |
| **Architecture** | Should authority itself move? |

***Six kinds of governance change, separated. That separation repeatedly prevented errors earlier versions of the programme would have made — and the correction above is a seventh instance of it working: promoting a disposition to an ontology is an ONTOLOGY change made on EVIDENCE-level support.***

**FINAL STATE 2026-07-31: PKS PHASE II GOVERNANCE BASELINE COMPLETE, with the completion claim and the terminal-state ontology both scoped to what the evidence supports.**

## ⭐ CLOSING — the meta-principle carried forward · Phase II closed (2026-07-31)

> ## **EVERY GOVERNANCE ACT SHOULD ESTABLISH ONLY THE STRONGEST CLAIM THAT ITS EVIDENCE PRESENTLY SUPPORTS.**

**A QUALITY CRITERION, not a methodology step** *(the Authority's own framing)*. **It explains almost every refinement in Phase II:** confirmed vs strengthened · dissolved vs superseded · document vs rule vs binding · rationale vs ontology · readiness vs authorization · implementation vs adoption · observation vs methodology · disposition vs terminal state.

> ***Every one narrowed a claim. None changed an outcome. That is the criterion's signature: it costs rhetoric and buys accuracy.***

### The scope of what was completed
| This programme HAS | It has NOT |
|---|---|
| **exhausted the questions it set out to answer** | **finished governance** |

***Different statements. Any further substantial governance work begins as a NEW CYCLE with a NEW MANDATE — triggered by new evidence, new architectural needs, or operational experience — never by reopening questions already at lawful terminal states.***

**Recorded in the Integrity Model and in MEMORY.md so the criterion survives the session.**

## 🏁 PKS PHASE III OPERATIONAL VALIDATION CHARTER ISSUED (2026-07-31)

**`docs/implementation/PKS_Phase_III_Operational_Validation_Charter.md`.** Label collision-checked under PMR-10. **A NEW GOVERNANCE ACT — it establishes a new cycle's purpose and success criteria, expressly not a continuation of Phase II.**

### Guiding principle
> **Sixteen reviews, five adoptions, and a frozen baseline establish that the methodology is INTERNALLY SOUND. They establish NOTHING about whether it WORKS.**

**Linked to the one dimension Phase II left at zero:** *Operational Evidence stands at ZERO-INDEPENDENT because **a certification dimension derives credibility from being outside the unilateral control of the programme it certifies — only USE can move it.***

### Purpose · Scope · Feedback path
Taken from the mandate as issued. **Feedback path unchanged from Phase II's — no new instrument created.**

### ⚠️ TWO GUARDS added under the programme's own discipline
**GUARD 1 — the criteria must ADMIT NEGATIVE FINDINGS.** *Every success criterion in the mandate is written in the improvement direction.*
> ***A validation that can only observe success is not a validation.***

Each negative form is now stated: decisions applied **inconsistently or bypassed** · change control **ambiguous or routed around** · ***reviews SLOWER or MORE OPAQUE than before the governance existed*** · disagreements **resolved despite it, or stalled by it** · the baseline **generating theoretical concerns that never materialize**.

**GUARD 2 — these are OBSERVATIONS, not TARGETS.** ***A criterion treated as a target can be met by changing behaviour to satisfy the measure. "Reviews become faster" is evidence when observed and a distortion when pursued.***

### §5.2 — the HONEST FAILURE DIRECTION named in advance
> ### **The baseline's most likely failure is not being WRONG. It is being TOO EXPENSIVE FOR THE VALUE IT DELIVERS — correct governance that no one can afford to follow.**

*Named because the programme's own governed question requires it, and because a validation that has not stated what would falsify it can only accumulate confirmations.*

### §7 — operating rules
Baseline FROZEN (changes via evidence → MCA → CDR → issuance) · **backlog items WAIT until they have a CONSTITUTIONAL REASON TO MOVE** · deferrals stay deferred on their own conditions · administrative work is now **normal maintenance, not constitutional work** · and:
> ⛔ ***If a Phase III act produces a governance artifact without producing OPERATIONAL EVIDENCE, that act has FAILED THE MANDATE — regardless of the artifact's quality.***

### §8
**Operational use begins. No further governance act is required to start.** ***The next governance commission should be triggered by evidence gathered during operational work — not by this charter, and not by the remaining backlog.***

## Summary

Disposed ER-F1/ER-F2 (accept both, remedies applied), recorded Discipline Amendment 4, corrected a chronology error in my own audit records, and recorded the Authority's refinements: retention as the observability condition for lawful maintenance, the five governance domains with their orthogonality precision, the full admission chain, and the polished-history principle.

## ⚠️ Chronology correction — self-inflicted, in acts about chronology

**The ER-F1/ER-F2 disposition, Discipline Amendment 4 and the Execution Record's maintenance note were all stamped `2026-07-30`. They were executed on `2026-07-31`.** **Corrected in all four artifacts** (ER-1 review §14 + its currency row + traceability · the Discipline's Amendment 4 heading · the Execution Record's maintained-header note).

***Recorded plainly rather than quietly fixed: a mis-dated stamp in an execution ledger is the same defect class as ER-F1 — a claim about time that does not match the time — and it was committed by the act that repaired ER-F1. The correction is metadata; no substance moved.***

## Completed

- **ER-F1 + ER-F2 — ACCEPTED, remedies APPLIED** (see 2026-07-30 log for the full record). Classified **MAINTENANCE**; zero segment bodies touched; original header text retained verbatim; §3/§8 preserved; nothing renumbered.
- **Self-application:** ER-1's own header annotated (frozen artifact → annotation), qualifier adjacent to the lapsed claim.
- **Discipline Amendment 4** — recognition ≠ agreement ≠ adoption, GOVERNED on five cross-context data.

## Decisions

**1. ⭐ RETENTION is the OBSERVABILITY CONDITION for lawful maintenance** *(Authority formulation, stronger than mine and adopted in its place)*:
> **Without retention of the superseded text, maintenance is OBSERVATIONALLY INDISTINGUISHABLE from rewriting history.**

**Four properties hold together with retention — the full test:** current state visible · **historical state immediately RECONSTRUCTIBLE** · no sealed segment changed · chronology intact. ***A maintainer who cannot be distinguished from a rewriter has not earned the distinction, however honest the intent. Retention makes the claim checkable by a reader who does not trust the maintainer.***

**2. THE FIVE GOVERNANCE DOMAINS recorded** (knowledge · execution · archival · methodology · meta-governance) — ***domains, NOT review families, NOT contracts, NOT emphases: different APPLICATIONS of one discipline, not expansions of the taxonomy.***

**⚠️ Orthogonality precision recorded because the collision is real:** there are **five review families** and **five governance domains**, and ***the coincidence of count is ACCIDENTAL — the axes are orthogonal.*** A family classifies **what a review EXAMINES**; a domain classifies **what governance ACTIVITY is performed**. ***"Execution" appears in both and means different things.*** **ER-1 is the proof: a PRESERVATION-family review operating in the ARCHIVAL and EXECUTION domains at once.**

**3. Amendment 4 extended with the FULL ADMISSION CHAIN:** Recognition → Agreement → Methodological admission (if applicable) → Assessment → Authority disposition → Adoption.
> **AGREEMENT DOES NOT POSSESS LEGISLATIVE FORCE** — regardless of source (reviewer, architect, or Authority). *Otherwise governance collapses into preference.*

***PMR-8 is the load-bearing evidence precisely because immediate adoption was the easiest path: a gate is only demonstrable when passing through it costs the party who could have skipped it.***

**4. A POLISHED HISTORY CONCEALS THE DECISION POINT** *(Authority)* — an auditable history shows future readers **both** the inconsistency **and** the reasoning that resolved it. ***The value of the record is not that it is clean but that a later reader can find where judgment was exercised and check it.***

## Problems

**The Authority identified its own contradictory instruction and confirmed the second statement (the evidential boundary) was correct while the first overstepped it.** *Recorded because it validates the handling: the ambiguity was declared rather than silently resolved, and the resolution supplied the missing evidence inside the same act.*

- **PMR-7 ADOPT IN PART + SPLIT** — **7a (two-level determinism VOCABULARY) ADOPTED** with the CI-1 constraint carried into the baseline (*the vocabulary BOUNDS CI-1; it does not fault it*); **7b (planning control) and 7c (verification control) DEFERRED at n=1 each.** *Deferral condition: a second instance PER CONTROL — the existing two data are one defect at two stages, so re-observing it would discharge neither.* **PMR-7 not renumbered: 7a/7b/7c EXTEND its identity.**
- **PMR-2 DEFER** on one verification (are AFV-1's four over-strengthenings instances?). **⚠️ The verification is NOT commissioned here — *a CDR that commissioned its own discharge conditions would be setting its own agenda.***
- **PMR-3 DEFER on a DATA condition (n≥2 simpler contexts), not a date.** ***Recorded as UNDECIDABLE FROM HERE:*** a distinction needing comparative data cannot be established by the party holding only one side of it.
- **PMR-1 DECLINE IN PART + DEFER RESIDUE** — the finding-vocabulary portion **declined as discharged**, because ***a duplicated control is worse than an absent one: two homes for one rule permit divergence.*** Routing-class residue deferred pending evidence of an actual mis-routing. *Declined explicitly, not allowed to lapse silently.*
- **PMR-6 DECLINE** on MCA-R1's strengthened grounds.

**⭐ §1 CIRCULARITY CLEARED BEFORE ANY ADOPTION COULD STAND:** two adoptions rest on the determination that the filter is *stated for controls* — which is also PMR-8's substance, and PMR-8 is unassessed. **Determination: MCA-R1 INTERPRETED an existing control's scope (lawful); PMR-8 would MANDATE that interpretation as procedure (methodology change).** ***An assessment that must not interpret the rule it applies cannot apply it at all.*** **The adoptions rest on the existing filter correctly read; PMR-8 gains nothing.**

**All four named distinctions verified as HELD:** recognition⟂agreement⟂adoption (PMR-4's precedence withheld *despite* confirmation) · annotation⟂maintenance (a CDR decides, it does not edit) · preservation⟂methodology (adoption prospective only) · family⟂domain (this act is wholly methodology-governance, performed no review).

**The arithmetic of restraint, with its counter-reading:** *seven candidates, one adopted whole, two adopted in part with their unsupported halves withheld, two deferred on conditions time cannot discharge, two declined — six of seven argued for by someone in this programme, six of seven held short of full adoption.* **⚠️ But 1-of-7 is ALSO consistent with a filter too strict to admit good practice. Both readings recorded, NEITHER adopted — *a CDR that judged its own filter would be certifying the instrument by which it decides.***

**Consequences: SDM v1 / EOP v1 remain OPERATIVE.** *Decision ≠ execution.* **Three adoptions require an AMENDED BASELINE, which this act does NOT issue.** Certification state unchanged; **Operational Evidence remains at zero — *an adoption that appeared to move it would be the strongest sign the dimension was measuring the wrong thing.***

**Open and NOT commissioned here: the AFV-1 over-strengthening verification · the amended-baseline issuance · PMR-8's assessment · MCA-R1's explicit closure.**

## MCA-R1 CLOSED · SDM v1.1 / EOP v1.1 ISSUED · item 3 found STRUCTURALLY BLOCKED (2026-07-31)

### Item 1 — MCA-R1 CLOSED (Authority act)
**Closed on the Authority's ground: *"ceremonies matter because they make governance state explicit."*** **Verdicts unchanged by closure** — *closure records that the assessment is finished; it does not ratify or revise a verdict.* **Status row annotated (frozen record → annotation, qualifier adjacent to the lapsed claim).** ***It was the only completed instrument in the corpus without an explicit closure statement, while every closed review carried one — and a governance state that must be INFERRED is not explicit, however obvious the inference.*** **Reserved to the Authority, not self-issued: an assessment that closed itself would be the same defect class as one that certified itself.**

### Item 2 — SDM v1.1 / EOP v1.1 ISSUED (`PKS_Phase_II_Methodology_Baseline_v1_1.md`)

**⭐ STRUCTURAL FACT DISCOVERED, and it determined the act's form: SDM v1 / EOP v1 have NO STANDALONE DOCUMENT.** The original CDR froze them *"each as defined by the existing governed record"* — the approved plan as executed, plus MCR-1..MCR-6 recorded inside the CDR. ***There is no baseline TEXT to amend; the baseline is a DEFINITION BY REFERENCE across several artifacts.***

**So v1.1 takes the same form v1's definition took (v1's governed record + the CDR-R1 adoptions, enumerated), and EXPRESSLY DECLINES TO CONSOLIDATE:** ***consolidating would change HOW THE BASELINE EXISTS — a structural change to the governance architecture, not an issuance. This act has authority to issue adoptions, not to restructure the object it issues them into.***

**Three adoptions issued, nothing else:**
- **Assembly Fidelity Rule — VERBATIM.** ***An issuance that polished the text it was issuing would breach the rule at the moment of enacting it.*** GOVERNED; prospective only.
- **Strategic/Tactical boundary — core-principle STATUS, with the NO-PRECEDENCE exclusion written INTO the baseline text** so a later citation cannot infer precedence from the label.
- **Two-level determinism VOCABULARY**, with the CI-1 bounding constraint carried in, and **7b/7c expressly excluded** — ***a baseline that shipped the vocabulary's implications would adopt by implication what the CDR deferred explicitly.***

**v1 not edited or retracted — forward-only. MCR-1..6 unchanged. Certification state unchanged; Operational Evidence still zero-independent.**

### Item 3 — STRUCTURALLY BLOCKED, and the block is reported rather than worked around

**Three obstacles, all reserved to the Authority:**
1. **⚠️ A THREE-WAY NAMING COLLISION on "AFV":** **AFV-1** is a *promoted artifact* (Model→Architecture fidelity verification) and ***cannot be "run"*** · an **AFV-class verification of v1.1** would be a *new act* · the **PMR-2 discharge verification** (are AFV-1's four over-strengthenings composite-confidence instances?) is a *third, unrelated act*. **Commissioning "AFV-1" is ambiguous across all three.**
2. **⚠️ THERE IS NO INCORPORATED TEXT TO VERIFY AGAINST.** A fidelity check compares a derived text to its source; §1 shows the baseline is a definition by reference. ***Verifying v1.1's §2 would compare the register's text to the register's text — not a fidelity check but a tautology.***
3. **What IS verifiable now without consolidation:** did v1.1 add exactly the three adoptions and nothing else · did it adopt anything by implication that the CDR deferred. **What REQUIRES consolidation first:** faithful incorporation into a baseline document · unintended strengthening beyond sources.

> **RECOMMENDATION OFFERED, NOT ACTED ON: decide whether SDM/EOP should be CONSOLIDATED into a baseline text. If yes → its own controlled-change act, and an AFV-class verification follows naturally. If no → item 3 collapses into the cheap scope check, commissionable immediately.** ***Choosing would be restructuring the baseline on my own authority.***

### Item 4 — PMR-8's MCA: NOT started. It requires its own commission; nothing in this session authorized it.

### Principle recorded
**Generalized from the readiness-gate observation the Authority asked to preserve:**
> **A governance gate has value only while the governed act is still CONTINGENT.** After the decision is taken, a gate can influence **issuance · publication · execution** — never the decision itself.

***This distinguishes DECISION governance from PUBLICATION governance*** — and it is why the CDR-R1 readiness check was applied honestly to issuance rather than pretended onto drafting.

## ⛔ ISV-1 — Issuance Scope Verification of v1.1: MAJOR DEFECT FOUND, AND IT WAS MINE (2026-07-31)

**`PKS_Phase_II_ISV_1_Issuance_Scope_Verification.md`. Authority ruling adopted: NOT an AFV — for a definition-by-reference baseline there is no derived text, so a classical AFV does not exist for this structure.**

**Independence defect DECLARED BEFORE any result: I authored v1.1 and verified it; RET-1 §5.3 measures self-verification at 0-FOR-2. Mitigation — every assertion is a re-runnable mechanical text comparison.** ***The mitigation does not cure the defect; it makes the defect cheap to test.***

### ⛔ ISV-F1 — MAJOR
**The Assembly Fidelity Rule was issued into the operative baseline in PARAPHRASE while the issuing act asserted it was VERBATIM.**
- **Authoritative (register):** *"…must be proposed **against the authoritative source and propagated through the established publication chain**."*
- **As issued:** *"…must be proposed and disposed, not applied in assembly."*

**Two substantive requirements DROPPED — a WEAKENING, not a stylistic variant:** where a proposal goes (the authoritative source) and that it must propagate through the publication chain.

> ***The rule prohibits modifying normative wording inherited from an authoritative source. Issuing it in modified wording breached the rule at the moment of enacting it — and the issuing act simultaneously asserted it had not.***

**⭐ THE PROPAGATION PATTERN IS THE RULE'S OWN EVIDENCE, REPRODUCED: the paraphrase ENTERED at MCA-R1, CASCADED through CDR-R1, landed in the baseline — THREE SITES.** ***PMR-5 was adopted on the evidence of an eleven-site restatement cascade and was then propagated by a three-site restatement cascade. The candidate's own failure mode consumed the candidate.*** **Clearest possible demonstration that the party restating normative text cannot detect its own restatement.**

**Remediation split by CLASS, not convenience — one defect, three artifacts, two mechanisms:**
- **SDM v1.1 (OPERATIVE) → CORRECTED** to the authoritative wording. *An operative baseline carrying wrong normative text is a LIVE defect; the correction executes CDR-R1's actual decision ("adopt verbatim") and decides nothing new.*
- **v1.1's false verbatim claim → RETAINED with the falsity marked.** ***An issuance record that deleted its own false claim would conceal the defect it caused.***
- **MCA-R1 · CDR-R1 (FROZEN) → ANNOTATED, not corrected** — *rewriting them would erase the evidence of the cascade.*

### ⚠️ A correction I owe on my own reasoning
**I argued that comparing v1.1's §2 to the register would be *"a tautology, not a fidelity check."* That was WRONG, and the check I dismissed as near-empty is the one that found this defect.** ***A comparison is only a tautology if the two texts are in fact identical — which is the very thing the comparison exists to establish. I mistook the intended outcome for the guaranteed one.***

### Other results
**7 of 8 assertions PASS** (only PMR-5 whole · PMR-4 partial with the no-precedence exclusion written INTO the text · PMR-7 partial with 7b/7c named and excluded so exclusion is verifiable not inferable · PMR-8 absent · no deferred item incorporated · no implicit adoption · no structural change).

**⚠️ §3 — A DEFECT IN THE VERIFICATION SPECIFICATION ITSELF: the assertion list said *"no wording strengthened"* and the defect found was a WEAKENING.** ***A scope verification testing only for strengthening is blind in one direction; the correct assertion is "no wording ALTERED" — an issuance can betray its source by saying LESS as easily as by saying MORE.*** *Recorded against the specification, not the Authority: the list predates knowing the risk's direction, and prior experience was all over-strengthening. **VR-2 — the one weakened governed term — was in the record and should have been the warning.*** **ISV-O1 carried, not adopted.**

**§4 — "Issuance Scope Verification" is an INSTRUMENT USED, not a CLASS ADMITTED.** Tested against all three stabilizers: not a review, not a new responsibility, n=1. ***Naming a verification is not admitting a construct. One use is an act; repeated use is a pattern; only a pattern with evidence of escape becomes a construct.***

**§5 — AFV naming collision QUEUED per Authority ruling ("not urgently — but eventually"), and expressly NOT resolved by inventing names for the other two.**

### Authority architectural ruling recorded (Integrity Model)
**Definition-by-reference ⟂ consolidated baselines, neither superior.** *Reference-based: **only ONE authoritative wording per governed statement, no copy that can drift**; trade-off: some fidelity verifications cease to be meaningful.*
> **The verification strategy adapts to the architecture, not the architecture to the verification strategy.**

**Two governing rulings: (1) do NOT consolidate merely to enable a verification — *governance defines the artifact structure; verification SERVES it*; (2) a classical fidelity verification does not exist for a reference-based baseline — register→register is identity, not fidelity.**

**⚠️ BOUNDED BY ISV-1's OWN RESULT: the ruling holds for the SHAPE of the verification, not its emptiness.** ***What is absent is a derived BASELINE; what is present and must always be checked is every QUOTATION drawn from the authoritative source.***

**Item 4 (PMR-8's independent MCA) NOT started — and it carries a circularity to settle first: an MCA assessing PMR-8 must decide whether to USE PMR-8's proposed procedure while assessing it.**

## Architectural question CLOSED · ISV naming BOUNDED · AFV collision to backlog (2026-07-31)

### ⚠️ Terminology ruling adopted — and it caught a gap between my claim and my conduct
**I wrote *"an instrument USED, not a class ADMITTED"* while minting a filename, a title and an `ISV-F1` identifier prefix.** ***That is the programme's quality-C defect exactly: disclaimers define INTENDED responsibility; wording determines EXERCISED responsibility.*** **The Authority was more conservative than I was, and was right.**

**Remedy: BOUNDED, not renamed** — the identifier-stability rule forbids renaming (*ledger identifiers are part of the evidence; clarify → alias → annotate, never renumber*). ISV-1's header now records that "ISV" is a **local act label**, not an admitted class, and that the governed phrasing is ***"perform a scope verification of the issuance."***

> ⭐ ***LESSON RECORDED: a name, once issued into the record, cannot be WITHDRAWN — only BOUNDED. Premature naming is permanent in a way premature silence is not.*** *That asymmetry is the strongest available argument for the Authority's conservatism, and it is now the recorded reason for the conservative default.*

### Decision 1 — TWO BASELINE ARCHITECTURES, recorded as a DISCOVERED property, not a proposal
**Model A MATERIALIZED** (decision → consolidated document → verification of the document; *decision → COPY → risk of transcription/synchronization/divergence*) ⟂ **Model B REFERENCE-DEFINED** (decision → definition by reference → governed baseline; ***no transcription step***).

*Evidence is operational: the original CDR already defined SDM v1/EOP v1 by reference, and v1.1 lawfully extended it without a consolidated document.* **Model B optimizes for a different RISK PROFILE — it removes an entire defect class, and in exchange fidelity verification of copied text becomes unnecessary because there is no copied text.**

> ### ⚠️ **QUALIFICATION FORCED BY ISV-F1: Model B eliminates transcription into the BASELINE — NOT into the RECORDS that decide and issue it.**

**ISV-F1 occurred entirely in that residual path: the rule's text was transcribed register → MCA-R1 → CDR-R1 → issuance, three times, one corrupted, even though the baseline holds no copy.** ***The transcription risk class was not removed; it was RELOCATED from the baseline to the decision chain, where nothing was checking it.*** **Practice consequence: under Model B every QUOTATION drawn from an authoritative source into a decision or issuance record must be compared against that source — precisely because the baseline no longer provides a second place to catch the error.**

### Decision 2 — VERIFICATION FOLLOWS THE ARTIFACT ARCHITECTURE
> **The strategy is selected BECAUSE OF the artifact's architecture, not because a technique already exists.**

**derived → fidelity · reference-defined → scope/completeness · immutable ledger → execution-evidence.** *This is the general form of the ruling that let the AFV question be DISSOLVED rather than answered.*

### The four settled conclusions (architectural question CLOSED)
1. Do **not** consolidate solely to enable verification. 2. Do **not** perform a tautological fidelity verification where no derived artifact exists. 3. Verify what actually **changed** — the issuance scope — with a verification appropriate to a reference-defined baseline. 4. Treat any future consolidation as an **independent** controlled-change decision, justified on its own merits, never as a workaround for verification.
> ***Let the architecture determine the verification, not the verification determine the architecture.***

### AFV collision → BACKLOG (not a PMR)
**Kept on the backlog per ruling, not prioritized over the queue.** *Ground: "one recommendation became impossible to execute because three legitimate interpretations existed" — **governance ambiguity, not stylistic ambiguity**.* **Trigger stated so it does not sit without a criterion: *if similar collisions appear elsewhere* — one collision is a backlog item, a second makes it a candidate.** **Not a PMR because it changes no future practice; and any normalization must proceed by ALIAS, since renaming is forbidden — *the collision can be made navigable but never erased.***

- *"No MCA, no CDR, no M7 performed"* and all of §7 → ✅ ***PERMANENTLY TRUE: these are statements about THIS ACT'S OWN SCOPE, not about the world. "This act performed no MCA" does not lapse when an MCA later occurs; only "no MCA exists" would.***

## CON-F1 SOURCE VERIFICATION (Authority-directed) — finding REFRAMED and ELEVATED; CON-F2 exposed (2026-07-31)

**The Authority reserved judgment and asked one question: does *"must keep them visible"* already exist as an implication of a governing source? Verified.**

### My original framing does NOT hold — the Authority's caution was correct
- ***"keep them visible" / "force placement" appear in NO artifact but the consolidation itself.***
- **The governing formulation is M6 §8: contested memberships are *"recorded, not assigned"* — a description of M6's OWN act, not an obligation on M7.** The Authority Disposition never addresses them.
- **BUT the obligation IS substantively derivable:** silently dropping a contested membership resolves it by omission, which the constitutional rule forbids. ***So it is verbally original but substantively carried — my "consolidation strengthened a staged obligation" framing was too strong.***

### ⛔ CON-F1 REFRAMED — MODERATE: §4 stages two INCOMPATIBLE obligations
- **§4-4:** *"unassigned; M7 must keep them visible, not force placement"*
- **§4-7:** *"Constraint: M7 models relationships between the **disposed** elements by reference"*
- **Contested memberships are UNASSIGNED = not disposed.** ***Item 4 requires M7 to carry elements item 7 places outside its scope, and the conflict is unmarked.***

> ***Not a strengthened obligation but an UNRESOLVABLE one — and it is the sharper form of the risk the commission actually asked about. "Are downstream stages genuinely staged, or subtly constrained?" Neither: one stage was subtly given a contradiction.***

### ⛔ CON-F2 — MODERATE · REPORTED, NOT DISPOSED, NOT FIXED · touches PROMOTED M7
**The conflict was resolved in execution BY OMISSION. Verified mechanically on `PKS_Phase_II_M7_Strategic_Relationship_Model.md`: occurrences of "Risk" = 0 · "Exception" = 0 · "Question" = 0.** **And M7 §3 explicitly cites *"Consolidation §4 input package (the staging this commission consumes)"* — *M7 cannot be said not to have received the instruction.*** M7's declared scope (*"among the disposed elements"*) shows it followed item 7.

**CONCLUDED:** the obligation was not discharged; M7 followed §4-7 over §4-4; a defensible reading exists on both sides.
**EXPRESSLY RESERVED TO THE AUTHORITY:** whether this is an **implicit resolution by omission** of a contested matter · whether M7 is defective or lawfully out of scope (*an unassigned element arguably has no relationships to classify — though "visible as unmodeled" would have cost one line*) · **whether the consolidation's §3 constitutional check survives the fact.**

> ***§3's check — "no open question was decided implicitly" — was true of every act UP TO the consolidation. It could not speak for what execution would later do with a contradiction the consolidation had staged.***

**Boundaries: nothing fixed; M7 is PROMOTED and was not amended.** ***No prior review caught this — not M7's relationship-integrity review, not KBI-1 at baseline scope, not the promotion readiness verification. It surfaced ONLY because the Authority declined to accept a finding on my framing and asked for the source.***

**CON-1's verdict row MAINTAINED with the superseded verdict RETAINED VERBATIM** — *retention is the observability condition: a verdict revised without its predecessor visible is indistinguishable from one that always said this.* **Boundary check 4 changed PASS → FAIL.**

### Recorded in the Method — the currency discriminator as a REUSABLE PRINCIPLE (Authority elevation)
> **Statements about the WORLD age. Statements about THIS ACT do not.**

*Complements the load-bearing test rather than replacing it: load-bearing asks whether a claim EXPLAINS a later act; this asks whether the claim was ever about anything but the act itself.* ***A claim can fail the load-bearing test and still never age.***

### Authority determinations recorded
Emphasis resolution AGREED (count stays TEN) · naming pattern AGREED as **observational, not methodological**, kept as BACKLOG evidence · **OBS-CON-1 ACCEPTED with the Authority's sharpening adopted: *the issue isn't quotation versus paraphrase — it's whether the artifact honestly signals which one it is*** · currency reasoning AGREED and elevated · stabilizer 3 AGREED as *architectural rather than accidental*.

**Revised standing verdict: review quality High · CON-F1 MODERATE (reframed) · CON-F2 MODERATE (reported) · OBS-CON-1 accepted.** *The provisional "Major findings: none" still holds — neither is Major — but "one Minor" no longer does.*

**State: THIRTEEN artifacts reviewed. CON-F1 + CON-F2 + OBS-CON-1 open. CON-F2 requires its own commission.**

## CON-1 scope-corrected and CLOSED · PMR-9 raised (2026-07-31)

### ⚠️ SCOPE CORRECTION applied at Authority direction — my §11 wording crossed the boundary it named
**Superseded (retained):** ~~*"the conflict was resolved in execution by omission"*~~ · ~~*"the obligation was not discharged"*~~. **Both concluded about M7'S CONDUCT.**

> ***CON-1 may conclude that the consolidation staged an incompatible package (A). Whether M7 resolved that incompatibility incorrectly (B) belongs to M7. The original wording collapsed A into B.***

**Corrected framing, descriptive not evaluative:** *the consolidation staged an input package whose obligations appear mutually incompatible, and later M7 execution followed one side of that package.* **Added to the reserved column: whether the obligation was *"discharged"* — *an evaluative judgment about M7's conduct, belonging to M7's own review.***

**CON-F1 also no longer characterized as "strengthening" the staged work, per Authority determination.**

### CON-1 CLOSED as a review; findings remain OPEN
**Lawful, not an exception: the promotion chain already distinguishes *Review Complete* from *Findings Dispositioned*.** ***A review that could not close until its findings were disposed would make every review hostage to a decision that is not its to take.*** **CON-F2 routed to a SEPARATE commission against the promoted artifact** — *"that preserves the separation between reviewing the staging artifact and reviewing the artifact that consumed the staged input."*

### ⭐ The review's most valuable output — Authority determination adopted
> **SOURCE VERIFICATION CHANGED THE ONTOLOGY OF THE FINDING.**

**Not Minor → Moderate. *"New obligation"* → *"internally inconsistent staged contract"* — a different defect CATEGORY:** class (minted an obligation → staged a contradiction) · subject (one item's citation → the relationship between two items) · remedy (cite or scope it → mark the conflict, which citation cannot fix).

> ***A severity error is a mis-measurement. An ontology error is a MIS-IDENTIFICATION — the remedy proposed under the first framing would not have touched the actual defect.***

### PMR-9 RAISED (not adopted)
> **Where a finding's CLASS depends on what a governing source actually says, the source shall be verified before the finding is CLASSIFIED — not merely before it is asserted.**

**CANDIDATE despite Authority endorsement — it prescribes how FUTURE REVIEWS are performed.** *An endorsement is not an adoption (Amendment 4).* **n=1 (CON-F1).** *RET-3's "verify support before calling it a defect" is recorded as a related but DISTINCT datum — verification before ASSERTION vs before CLASSIFICATION; whether it counts is an assessment question.*

**⚠️ Deliberately NOT bundled with PMR-8** — both are ordering rules from the same party on the same shape of insight (PMR-8: applicability before evidence, for assessments; PMR-9: source verification before classification, for reviews). ***Separated because CDR-R1 established that bundling distinct propositions under one identifier distorts the evidence count — PMR-7 had to be split into 7a/7b/7c for exactly that reason.***

**State: THIRTEEN artifacts reviewed · CON-1 CLOSED · CON-F1 + CON-F2 + OBS-CON-1 OPEN · register 9 candidates (PMR-8, PMR-9 unassessed) · CON-F2 needs its own commission against promoted M7.**

- **Remedy = ANNOTATION** (frozen closure artifact), placed **adjacent to §4** so a reader meets it where the staged obligations are. No section body altered.
- **⚠️ CALIBRATION the Authority required: the annotation records the incompatibility as *APPARENT*, not established** — *if a modeling convention already permits representing non-disposed elements as contextual or deferred participants, the apparent contradiction dissolves with no change to either artifact.* ***An annotation asserting a confirmed contradiction would decide, in the consolidation's record, the very question the M7 commission exists to answer.***

### OBS-CON-1 — ACCEPT as an observation; CARRIED, NOT remediated
*Basis: "OBS-CON-1 accepted as a useful review observation."* **No remediation — *an observation is never remediated.*** Nothing changes in the consolidation.

**Neither disposition touches: CON-F2 (open) · M7 (promoted, unamended) · governance or certification state · methodology (*PMR-9 gains nothing — acceptance of the evidence is not acceptance of the lesson*).**

### COMMISSION M7-CF2 ISSUED — against promoted M7
**⚠️ THE PREMISE IS UNESTABLISHED, and the commission's FIRST task is to test whether it exists at all.**

> **GOVERNING QUESTION (the Authority's own, prior to all others): does *"relationships between disposed elements"* implicitly permit representing NON-DISPOSED elements as CONTEXTUAL or DEFERRED participants?**
> **If YES → the apparent contradiction DISSOLVES, no artifact changes, CON-F2 closes as dissolved. If NO → the contradiction is real and only then do the downstream questions arise.**

***The ordering is the commission's binding constraint: a commission that began by asking whether M7 is defective would have ASSUMED the contradiction it was sent to test.***

**Reachable only if dissolution fails:** implicit resolution by omission? · M7 **defective, incomplete, or entirely lawful** (*the Authority's three-way framing preserved — "defective" is NOT the default*) · does the consolidation's §3 constitutional check survive? · is change control engaged?

**Evidence supplied so it isn't re-derived; boundaries set (must not re-review M7's model, reopen CBC dispositions, amend M7, adjudicate CON-F1, or propose methodology; must report rather than remedy).**

**⚠️ Recorded so the commission cannot inherit my error: *CON-F1's first framing was WRONG, and it was wrong because it classified before verifying the source. This commission is directed at an artifact whose defect is likewise unestablished, and should expect dissolution as a LIVE OUTCOME, not a formality.***

> **An MCA may evaluate PMR-8/PMR-9 under the EXISTING certified methodology, treating them as HYPOTHESES: *"Would adopting PMR-9 improve the methodology, based on evidence gathered under the CURRENT methodology?"***

**`docs/implementation/PKS_Phase_II_MC1_Certification_Inference_Review.md`.** **Preservation family · RETROSPECTIVE emphasis · object = CERTIFICATION ASSESSMENT.**
